made when the twelfth reservation spends quota. A grant requires at least ten
seconds of distinct, proven PCM after that checkpoint (or the prior grant), and
30 monotonic seconds after exhaustion/prior grant. Each tranche is granted only
when the current allowance is exhausted and a socket token can be reserved;
eligibility is rechecked before every extra reservation, including unused attempts
in an already granted tranche. The existing near-miss predicate requires the owner
to be nearest with distance in [0.65, 0.70), the existing 0.10 runner-up gap and no
contention. The completed-comparison ledger and any already-decided carried/manual row,
not attempts, failures or pending evidence, distinguish undecided voices.
Before rollover clears decision rows, retained quota snapshots a bounded decided
marker. An absent row remains ineligible until a current rebuilt near-miss permits
recovery; profile/donor awaits cannot turn an accepted carried owner into an
undecided voice or replace its multi-clip centroid with a recovery clip. The marker
supplies no identity authority or rejector proof, and resets with uncarried quota.
Roster re-evaluation alone never counts as a completed round. Current profile
distances are rechecked, so a later close/far result can change eligibility without
resetting attempts, tranches, freshness checkpoints or cooldowns;
there is no timer, automatic failed-request retry, refund, or socket exemption.
Freshness uses the ring's absolute append byte positions AND a capture-axis
high-watermark, with sample-aligned, non-overlapping span proof. Segment duration,
client/provider timestamps, arrival gaps, repeated pending windows, and overlapping
PCM spans cannot mint fresh seconds. Old prebuffered audio is excluded; a replaced
ring starts a new byte checkpoint while retaining the capture watermark and granted attempts. Reordered
windows can conservatively lose credit. State is bounded by the existing 128-voice
inventory, contains scalar metadata, and holds only a weak reference to the ring.
Unproven buffers fail closed. Grant consumption discards excess pre-grant credit;
a second grant needs another ten new seconds and another cooldown.

Recovery snapshots its selection boundary before observing new proof, pools
post-checkpoint ranges first up to ten seconds (including gapped phrases), and
uses older pending audio only when new physical PCM is below the evidence minimum.
The boundary remains available throughout the active tranche; advancing the proof
cursor cannot demote unreserved phrases. Grant checkpoints advance over all current
PCM only when post-checkpoint audio is selected for reservation and the increased
allowance covers the next attempt. Longer/newer intervals win within each pool,
using the newest end of long turns. The physical PCM
sample count is also capped at ten seconds even if malformed timestamp spans
overlap. Existing centroid eviction, thresholds/margins, manual authority,
owner contention and stale-generation fences still decide acceptance.

`omi_speaker_id_recovery_tranches_total{tranche="first|second"}` counts each
allowance grant exactly once, synchronously with its first admitted reservation.
`omi_speaker_id_recovery_acceptances_total{target="owner|person"}` counts the first
automatic acceptance per voice-generation after a successful recovery extraction;
it excludes manual labels, failed/stale extraction and contended/rejected results.
Later re-arbitration may accept that evidenced voice; repeated/reinstated acceptance
does not count twice. Carried state is not counted as a new recovery cohort.
`omi_speaker_id_recovery_denials_total{reason="proven_rejector|clearly_far|completed_not_near_owner"}`
counts each otherwise attempted extra reservation refused by eligibility, including
a grant request or an unused attempt in an existing tranche. `proven_rejector`
requires the unchanged three-round proof; `clearly_far` covers completed clear
rejection before that proof; `completed_not_near_owner` covers other completed
results outside the current calibrated near-miss predicate, including a retained
decided voice whose current row is absent during refresh. These refusals spend
no attempt/token and never advance the tranche checkpoint. Freshness, cooldown,
tranche/attempt ceilings and socket refusals are not counted in this eligibility
family; they retain the existing cap/legacy telemetry. Rates measure refusals,
not unique voices or causal GPU savings. These three recovery families add seven
counter series per target plus seven `_created` series; all label values are
preinitialized and closed, and no IDs are labels or new log payloads. Existing
#21181/#21235 budget/diagnostic and #21298 rejector counters keep their meanings.
Compare summed grants, first/second usage, eligibility denials and owner
recovery acceptance rates with voice-cap incidence and finalized outcomes on the
same instrumented pods. Acceptance/grants is descriptive, not a causal success
rate: a voice can use two grants, and acceptance can lag a grant. Lower exit counts
alone do not prove better recognition. Use a matched control and monitor false
acceptance/contention, latency and extraction request rates.

Worst-case additional GPU requests are six per qualifying voice-generation
(+50% versus its initial allowance), each at most ten seconds of PCM. Failed or
cancelled reservations spend this allowance too. All voices still share the
unchanged socket envelope below, so the socket's maximum request rate cannot rise;
actual utilization can rise within it.

The socket token bucket starts with 16 attempts, has capacity 16, and refills
at 240/3600 attempts per monotonic wall second (one every 15 seconds). Over `t`
hours since socket creation, attempts are bounded by `16 + 240*t`: at most 256
in the first hour and 240/hour in steady state, at no more than 10 seconds of
query PCM per attempt. Idle credit is capped at 16. Failures spend tokens and
reservation is synchronous before awaiting extraction; parallel voices share
the same bucket. Client audio timestamps cannot accelerate refill, and neither
conversation churn nor profile refresh resets the socket's tokens/refill clock. All
associated automatic voice state shares admission. Rollover prunes idle locks;
held/waited locks retire only after their last user finishes. Generation checks
prevent old work from publishing. Live unknown/ambiguous remains an abstention
when capacity is exhausted. The five-second decision floor, physical 0.5-second
floor and verification thresholds remain unchanged, as does WAL eligibility.

### Finished capture identity

Span resolution can use a verified subset of segments while other segments
lack placement. Unplaced segments abstain; inconsistent inventories, overlaps,
known coverage holes and raced blobs still refuse globally. Identity eligibility
uses actual distinct embedded duration independently of optional score logging.
Version-1 caches remain readable for grouping; entries without duration metadata
claim no owner evidence until verified audio supplies it.
When a segment has a proven audio window, an otherwise valid cache hit with
missing, zero or invalid measured evidence is refreshed from verified PCM under
the existing attempt/time limits. Nominal transcript duration is never promoted
to owner evidence. Unplaceable historical grouping remains cache-readable; a
failed refresh cannot claim new owner evidence. Valid measured cache entries
remain reusable without another embedding.

New evidence-policy cache writes use `speaker-embeddings.owner-evidence.v1.enc`
under the same encrypted, deletion-owned audio prefix. New readers prefer it,
then fall back to `speaker-embeddings.v1.enc`. Legacy objects stay untouched and
readable. Old pusher reads only the legacy filename: it cannot consume a new
short-evidence vector, or overwrite the new cache during reprocessing. This
is cache-policy isolation, not a retrofit of the old image's independent matching
behavior; old pusher still lacks the partial/late repair improvements.

After pusher finishes its private-cloud uploads, process-wide admission allows
at most two submitted/running late batches. Saturated admission can retain at
most two additional ID-only batches, each with at most 128 distinct candidates.
These wait asynchronously for 5, 15, 30, 60 and 120 seconds (230 seconds total),
then expire if admission is still unavailable. Waiting performs no Firestore,
audio, or model calls and borrows no executor thread. Submissions coalesce across
active and waiting keys; the handoff is atomic. Fresh eligibility, consent,
manual authority and the document revision are read only after admission.
The waiting path retains no PCM and changes no audio retention policy. Each admitted batch scans
at most 128 distinct conversations and selects at most eight eligible rows,
including all deferred completion rechecks; admission waits spend none of those passes. Rows still `in_progress` or
`processing` retain their attempt: async waits of 5, 15, 30, 60 and 120 seconds
re-read only those rows (at most six metadata snapshots per candidate, 768 per
batch). Waits borrow no executor thread but retain the batch's admission slot.
The eight-pass ceiling is shared across every round; a row that enters repair
is removed from subsequent rounds even if matching or CAS fails. Rechecks use
the freshly completed row's revision, never the earlier processing snapshot.
Overlapping drain submissions coalesce per process while a candidate is active;
the active set is bounded by two batches of 128 and cleared on task completion.
This is an in-process handoff, not a durable job: completion after the 230 seconds
of waits, process shutdown, or slot refusal can still lose this retry.
Selection follows eligibility, so ineligible early rows do not consume the eight
passes. The snapshot is reused for the revision CAS. Each pass has a five-second
advisory elapsed budget and at most 24 model attempts, including failures; no
late pass repairs enrollment audio. Cancellation of an async waiter cannot free
admission while its storage thread is still running. SDK/network timeouts are
not a hard five-second deadline.
It changes only transcript identity and speaker resolution on completed,
unlocked pendant/WAL rows. The commit compares the read document revision and
reapplies manual authority; intervening edits defeat it. It performs no notes,
task or lifecycle replay, and desktop/phone channel paths remain excluded.

`capture_evidence.reason=missing_source_position` refers to absent/unusable
client source-frame receipts, not absence of backend capture windows. Flutter
always sends source-frame receipts for supported WAL frames. Listen
and pusher must not synthesize client capture roots or frame ordinals. The
span-storage producer and unconditional span-resolution consumer
permit identity placement independently of client receipts.

Owner acquisition is plan-independent and shared by listen, sync/backfill and
finished-conversation resolution. Validate vectors before comparison; failures
reading names, entitlements or paid people cannot erase a loaded owner. Legacy
enrollment-audio recovery publishes only while the existing owner timestamp
and missing vector remain unchanged, with account deletion fenced in the same
transaction. It never learns from an automatic recognition decision. Live
requests that explicitly exclude a profile, onboarding and channel-authored
capture remain excluded. Eligible sockets retain the bounded matcher loop so
a later confirmed profile can be observed on rotation or a bounded load retry.
Failed acquisition checks its monotonic deadline on both dequeue and idle paths.
Per socket, at most eight failed loads and seven owner-only retries are allowed,
with 30/60/120/240/480-second capped backoff; enrollment-audio repair has a separate
three-attempt ceiling and 30/120/480-second backoff. Owner retries never reload
paid people or entitlement. These counters survive rollover. A newly acquired
owner rechecks retained verified evidence against current prints and receipts,
without requesting more embeddings when the voice quota is already spent.

Short-fragment inheritance is bound to `(speaker_id_scope, speaker_id)`.
Acoustically contradicted provider voices cannot assign an unembedded short
reply by majority; automatic identity on such a reply is withdrawn. Manual
receipt authority still wins. A short distinct provider voice without its own
compatible scoped votes stays unknown, even beside a known owner in the same
scope; temporal proximity never supplies identity evidence. An existing owner
accept on that short voice survives absent votes: only incompatible acoustic
votes establish contradiction. Manual merge reallocates automatic-only sources through the
existing allocator, retaining absolute capture positions. Sources containing
manual authority retain their numeric ids; full cross-source receipt migration
remains outside this patch and is a merge residual.

Free-desktop deterministic minimum paths still emit no managed calls. This
patch does not add a diarizer pass before those admission exits; unmatched free
desktop captures and budget-skipped custom-STT captures remain a residual.

[Owner continuity across rollover and reconnect](owner-continuity.md) documents
the complete carry/drop reasons, encrypted handoff lifetime, acoustic guards,
and metric denominators.

Rollover rechecks the donor after profile loading and reevaluates the retained centroid
against the current eligible prints. Late manual rejection, deletion, lock, or a new
competing print defeats automatic carry; distinct covered audio remains reserved.

The late-audio identity transaction also reads the account deletion marker before
writing. Its placement coverage index is initialized even when no segment is long
enough to embed, so short-only conversations abstain without an unbound index.

Late identity persistence lives in `database/speaker_resolution_updates.py` so
unrelated conversation database reads do not import its deletion-marker graph.
New v1 cache writes record the already-supported optional evidence duration even
when score instrumentation is off. Historical entries keep their grouping and
PCM semantics; a missing duration cannot support a new positive or negative.
Unknown identity results preserve existing identity fields regardless of whether
the accept came from capture or an earlier partial conversation pass. Grouping
can still rewrite speaker IDs and scopes. The shared projection also retains an
existing owner when a person-only roster accepts someone else or returns no match:
without a validated owner print, neither an owner comparison nor the enrolled
runner-up margin was established. An absent, invalid, or failed owner read supplies
no revocation authority. The reduced-roster decision is suppressed for the whole
resolved voice: existing labels survive and unlabeled members remain unlabeled.
Explicit manual decisions remain authoritative.
This does not change scored rejection with an owner-inclusive roster, owner
contention, multi-vote acoustic contradiction, or the conservative withdrawal of
multiple independent sync owner claims on the no-resolution path.

Missing owner-vector publication is isolated in `database/owner_profile_updates.py`.
Both new identity persistence helpers pin the customer data plane and read its
account-deletion marker in the same transaction; no compute-plane fallback is used.

The late-audio retry excludes both `phone` and `phone_call`, as well as desktop,
to preserve channel-authored identity and the free-desktop processing boundary.

Participant grouping marked `resolved` does not by itself suppress the late
identity retry: only a resolved row already containing an owner label is skipped.
A completed owner miss can therefore use newly available audio/profile evidence.

`omi_owner_identity_repair_total{outcome}` counts identity changes in successful
late CAS commits (`owner_added`, `owner_removed`, `identity_updated`). It compares
decoded transaction-current segments to the actual encoded write after manual
authority has been reapplied. Missing legacy segment ids use the same stable ids
as conversation reads. Unchanged identities emit nothing, even when resolution
metadata is updated. The observer runs once after transaction success. It does not
observe ordinary finalization again. The existing conversation counter/share
remain first-finalization snapshots. Metric failure cannot change a committed
repair into a reported storage failure.

Owner work has lifetime socket bounds: seven retry owner reads, at most three
recovery-state reads, three two-document repair transactions, three lost-CAS
owner rereads, and seven retained-evidence receipt reads. This is at most 26
additional logical document reads per socket, or 50 when every repair transaction
uses all five Firestore transaction attempts. Normal per-conversation profile,
people/projection, entitlement and name reads are separate. The bounded owner
work does not replenish after a session-hour. Live audio adds at most 256 model
attempts plus three owner-enrollment repairs per socket (existing paid-person
repair is separate). Late audio adds at most 192 model attempts per admitted
batch and zero enrollment repairs, with at most two active batches and two
ID-only admission waiters per process. At most 512 distinct `(uid, conversation)` keys
are tracked; only the 256 active candidate keys can own executor work. Waiting
can improve utilization of the existing two slots, without adding GPU concurrency.

### Relevance decision analytics

After successful processing persistence, the final relevance decision is enqueued
through the shared backend PostHog helper as `Relevance Decision Recorded`.
Properties are only `conversation_id`, enum-like `reason`, optional `arm`, and
boolean `discarded`; no transcript or user content is emitted. Deferred desktop,
deterministic minimum, and enriched processing each emit once per stored decision,
after the persistence fence. Capture failures never fail processing. This bridge
supports per-conversation joins to opens going forward (EXP-004 closure); it does
not backfill the stopped keep-all sample or change intake, promotion, or relevance.

Recovery verification fingerprints the original stored transcript and audio ids.
Both kept and explicitly discarded recovery results preserve those capture fields
and their speaker metadata transactionally. Model normalization must not rewrite
the raw encoding or replace audio appended after the processing snapshot; only
structured enrichment and lifecycle fields belong to the recovery result.

Verification refusals still page critically for content shrinkage, job-binding
mismatches, and terminal failures. Only the sweep that wins the cursor CAS emits
those pages; observers that lose the same cursor generation cannot multiply a
single refusal into a fleet-wide alarm burst. Standalone verification pages
immediately. Recovery never regenerates admitted audio from old chunk snapshots.

Owner continuity decisions use one final Firestore transaction snapshot for the
receiving receipt, original acoustic donor and immediate rollover donor (at most
three projected documents). Live arbitration/publication runs synchronously after
the snapshot and generation check, so a receiving correction during earlier
awaits cannot be replaced by a stale automatic owner suggestion.

Snapshot unavailability is kept separate from the receiving receipt. A failed
final transaction passes only already-known receiving rejections/retractions.
One publication gate stops manual, automatic, carried and shortened owner naming
and continuity renewal whenever final authority is unavailable.

### Live capture inactivity observation

`omi_listen_live_no_audio_sessions` counts open backend-STT sockets in either
state: no accepted, decoded frame within 300 seconds of admission; or, after
the first decoded frame, no raw byte frame longer than two bytes for 300 seconds.
The 10-second heartbeat samples the existing receive timestamp and resets a
monotonic timer only when byte activity changes. Control messages do not reset
it. Audio arrival/resumption or teardown removes the socket. Custom STT is
excluded because it sends client transcripts. The gauge has no customer labels
and starts at zero; a transition logs `omi_listen_capture_idle`. This observes
inactivity without forcing a disconnect. Intentional idle clients can contribute.
It cannot see a pendant wedge when the app never opens a backend socket. After
the first accepted frame, undecodable/unknown-channel frames count as raw byte
activity; the decode/drop metrics cover those separate cases. Calibrate fleet
alert thresholds against the idle baseline before paging.

## Owner identity attempt funnel

`omi_owner_identity_retry_total{pass,stage,reason}` observes the existing speaker
resolver without changing placement, identity, cache or persistence policy.
`pass="first"` is a first-finalization call that reaches the shared resolver
(using the existing owner-outcome `already_observed` test). A reprocess already
observed by that test emits no first-pass funnel. Earlier processing admission
returns do not reach this counter. `pass="late"` is a pusher drain retry candidate,
including its bounded completion rechecks. A processing row is unfinished, not
an immediate terminal `skipped/not_completed`; that terminal is emitted once
only when the recheck ceiling is exhausted or the row cannot reach completion.
Rechecks do not increment `entry`, and legacy coalesced active submissions emit nothing. The optional UID queue counts
in-flight duplicates as `deferred/in_flight` without another entry or skip.
Suppressed terminal resubmissions emit a matching `entry/candidate` and
`skipped/cohort_seen`, preserving skipped/entry accounting.
Another batch after completion may count the same conversation again. These
are attempts, not unique conversations or evidence of owner speech.
The counter cannot reconstruct historical failures or identify an account.

The funnel `(stage,reason)` pairs are closed:

| Stage | Reasons | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| entry | candidate | Known deduplicated candidates, before slot/scan admission; first passes at resolver entry. |
| eligible | candidate | Late snapshot passed existing retry eligibility and ID check; first pass has a nonempty conversation model. |
| deferred | slot_limit, coalesced, in_flight | Candidates retained in legacy admission waiters or merged into a UID cohort; retained pending IDs read no snapshot or audio. |
| skipped | slot_limit, queue_limit, queue_expired, cancelled, cohort_seen, scan_limit, pass_limit, missing, not_completed, deleted, discarded, locked, channel_source, private_cloud_disabled, missing_revision, already_resolved_owner, id_mismatch, empty_transcript, manual_receipt_unavailable, read_error, processing_error, submission_error | One terminal skip; capacity reasons mean the candidate was not scanned, not that its audio was ineligible. |
| cas_refused | missing, account_deleting, deleted, discarded, locked, not_completed, revision_changed | Late-only terminal verdict from the actual transaction snapshot, emitted after the outer transaction returns. |
| cas_committed | done | Late-only terminal commit, including canonical identity no-op; transaction callback retries do not increment. |

Admission deferral uses the same closed retry funnel: `pass=late,stage=deferred,reason=slot_limit`
counts retained candidates. Full waiting capacity, expiry and cancellation
before admission emit
`stage=skipped,reason=slot_limit`; coalesced duplicates add no entry. This adds
one bounded stage/reason pair; with optional coalescing there are 67 pairs, at most 134 series across both passes;
UID and conversation IDs remain outside Prometheus labels. Deferred admission
counts are not commits or owner additions. Terminal waiters remove their keys
under the admission lock before recording the skip; later callbacks release only
their waiting permit and cannot erase a same-turn resubmission. Cancellation,
including before the waiter starts, records one skip and releases only its
metadata reservation; handed-off batches add no admission skip. A cancelled active coordinator still
retains its slot until the storage future finishes.

`OWNER_IDENTITY_RETRY_COALESCE_ENABLED=true` opts into a process-local UID
queue; unset/false retains the two batches plus two waiters above. At most 16
UID cohorts retain IDs, each with one active batch, at most 128 distinct IDs
(including already scanned IDs for dedupe) and eight eligible attempts across
all merged submissions. Workers still share the existing two process slots.
New IDs for a retained UID merge into its pending set (`deferred/coalesced`);
duplicates still pending or owned by a worker emit `deferred/in_flight`, with no
entry or skip. Duplicates that have exited the worker (including read_error,
processing_error or not_completed) emit one matching entry and `skipped/cohort_seen`.
Dedupe retains the candidate/read ceilings; it does not silently imply recovery. New UIDs beyond the cap emit
`skipped/queue_limit`. Admission expires after 230 seconds; completion rechecks
share a separate 230-second deadline across merged batches. New submissions
extend neither deadline nor the candidate/pass budgets. New pending IDs wake
completion waits and run fresh-ID-only rounds while older processing IDs retain
their scheduled recheck. Arrival wakes consume no completion recheck slot and
borrow no additional pass budget; each older row still has at most five timed
rechecks within the original deadline. Slot release (including worker-thread cleanup) wakes the oldest
waiting cohort; every admission checks FIFO arrival order. The original poll
intervals remain a fallback if a release signal is missed. Expired queued IDs emit
`skipped/queue_expired`; cancellation emits `skipped/cancelled` for pending IDs
and unfinished worker results, once, retaining UID/slot ownership until the
shielded storage worker finishes. No transcript/audio is retained by the queue,
and waiting borrows no executor thread. Overflow, expiry and pod termination
can still lose work: this queue is bounded, not durable. Roll back by setting the
flag false and restarting the process; no stored schema changes are involved.

Canary readout: compare `skipped/queue_limit`, `skipped/queue_expired`,
`skipped/cancelled`, `deferred/coalesced`, `eligible/candidate` and
`cas_committed/done`, plus `skipped/cohort_seen`, pass/read costs and owner_added.
`skipped/slot_limit` is a legacy terminal counter and goes quiet with this flag
on; it cannot measure recovery in the new queue.

The only scheduler caller is pusher's session-end audio drain after an uploaded
manifest update. Its False return is not retried. A later upload session can
submit the same conversation again, but no timer/sweep guarantees that trigger.
`slot_limit` also includes old admission waiter expiry/cancellation. These
aggregate attempt counters cannot count distinct missed conversations or prove
that a skipped conversation was never retried.

`resolver_exit` has exactly 30 primary reasons: `resolved`, `partial`, `disabled`,
`no_audio`, `all_short`, `no_eligible`, `no_vectors`, `global_manifest_ambiguity`,
`zero_text_window`, `invalid_text_window`, `uncovered_window`,
`manifest_unvalidated`, `capture_coverage_hole`, `placement_deadline`,
`no_proven_window`, `listing_limit`, `inventory_metadata_invalid`,
`manifest_inventory_invalid`, `inventory_count_mismatch`,
`inventory_pair_mismatch`, `blob_missing`, `blob_read_failed`, `blob_decode_failed`,
`read_budget`, `decoded_duration_mismatch`, `decoded_overlap`,
`decoded_coverage_hole`, `embedding_budget`, `embedding_failed`, `other`.

Exactly one exit is emitted for each nonempty resolver call in a traced pass.
It describes the first executed terminal gate. Placement deadline returns before
later inventory checks. At the existing global inventory veto, attribution is
ambiguity → zero/other invalid text window → uncovered text window (only with a
validated manifest) → unvalidated manifest → capture coverage hole. Only after
those gates pass does the actual inventory/read proof run; its checks retain
execution order: read budget/listing, object metadata, manifest inventory,
count/pair equality, blob fetch/decode, decoded duration, overlap, coverage.
No clock, storage, embedding, eligibility or proof read is added to classify it.
`read_budget` includes the existing download/byte/deadline/transient-listing
budget refusal, not proof that audio is absent. `no_proven_window` includes
missing origin/provider position, explicit unplaced alignment and legacy-clock
refusal. An unvalidated manifest never counts as a demonstrated coverage hole.
Embedding failure/budget are primary only if no resolution is produced; a
usable partial resolution emits `partial`. Disabling resolution emits `disabled`.

There are 58 allowed pairs per pass, at most 116 counter children per process;
children are created only on observation and collector registration is reload
safe. No user, conversation, segment, speaker, exception or scope enters labels.
Late `resolver_exit` is intermediate: unavailable/no-op resolution may still
commit. Do not add it to late terminal counts. Late terminals are `skipped` +
`cas_refused` + `cas_committed`; entry minus terminals measures unfinished
attempts across interval boundaries. First resolver exits end acoustic attempts,
not the eventual summary/persistence attempt. Compare first and late separately.
`omi_owner_identity_repair_total` still counts only committed canonical identity
changes, and `omi_owner_recognition_conversations_total` still counts first
finalization outcomes. Neither existing counter changes its unit or emission.

Deploy pusher for late and pusher-hosted first passes; deploy backend/listen and
Cloud Run backend, backend-sync and backend-sync-backfill for their first-pass
coverage. No new flags, indexes, stored fields, routes or app release are needed.
This telemetry adds no retries, audio downloads, embedding calls or profile
reads. Mixed image versions are safe; older images simply emit no new samples.

## Dark at-cap choice contract

`HELD_CONTENT_ENABLED` defaults to `false`; `X-Omi-Held-Content: 1` is also
required for `GET` and `PUT /v1/users/budget-choice`. Missing/unknown capabilities
receive 404 before route auth and any choice or allowance read for a valid body.
Malformed PUT JSON can return 422 before dependencies; it performs no Firestore reads. This foundation does not change
sync/listen admission or existing `is_locked` rows.

The API uses the existing UTC calendar-month usage bucket (including annual
subscriptions). One metadata-only document at
`users/{uid}/budget_choice/current` holds the period, `on_device` or `upgrade`,
and `dont_ask_again`. Identical writes are idempotent; conflicting writes in the
same period return 409. A new period logically expires the old choice and
replaces the same document, avoiding unbounded history. Delayed older-period
writes cannot overwrite a newer choice. Each transaction attempt also checks the
current UTC month after its read, so a request delayed by allowance resolution or
a transaction retry returns `409 budget_period_changed`, including identical
replays of an expired choice. The [hermetic custody acceptance and proposed
graduation ramp](runbooks/held-content-acceptance.md) covers this boundary.
`upgrade` records intent only; managed
processing still requires a positive answer from the existing allowance resolver.
A lookup failure or platform trial paywall is `unavailable`, never a cap hit.
`X-App-Platform` reaches the allowance resolver. Drifted BYOK recovery clears
invalid keys before this route; it cannot grant managed access. GET and PUT share
a boost-exempt six-per-minute UID limit, failing closed before monthly usage
reads when Redis is unavailable. The plans API alone owns storefront visibility;
this response makes no upgrade-availability claim. Responses are typed OpenAPI
models with generated wire DTOs, not manual client JSON adapters. The API adds no entitlement,
quota, provider, subscription, or captured-content mutation.

Do not enable this flag before capable clients, capture custody, recovery and
on-device continuation are implemented and verified. In particular, staged sync
audio expires after one day and is not a safe month-long hold store. Client WAL
custody must survive a hold until successful processing, within the existing
retention contract. No new client capability is advertised by this foundation.

### Flagged shared batch diarization

Production stage B binds authoritative diarization for the private
`transcription-final-pass` cohort on backend,
backend-sync, backend-sync-backfill and pusher through the shared Gateway
`/diarizer-batch` prefix. Sync is enabled from B onward; A disables both sync
and live authoritative promotion. Offline/backfill recordings bypass
pusher shadow admission, so acceptance checks the sync pipeline output. Exact env stages, caps,
acceptance gates and rollback: [batch cohort ramp](diarizer-batch-cohort.md).

`TRANSCRIPTION_FINAL_PASS_DIARIZER=legacy` preserves the current shadow and sync
paths. `pyannote_shadow` admits the private `transcription-final-pass` cohort or
`TRANSCRIPTION_FINAL_PASS_PERCENT`. The former UID env list remains a migration
fallback only. `TRANSCRIPTION_FINAL_PASS_BACKEND` selects
`pyannote` (default), `legacy_ahc`, or the contract-compatible `nemotron` HTTP
adapter. The latter requires its own serving endpoint and qualification.

The shadow worker retains the already decoded registered PCM with exact silence
padding between capture chunks; it sends no second storage download. Anonymous
turns, overlaps, centroid evidence, embedding-space identity and provider version
travel through one `DiarizationResult`. Community-1 exclusive turns determine each
word by maximum overlap; midpoint breaks a tie and gaps abstain. Centroids use the
same pinned WeSpeaker v2 space as enrollments; incompatible spaces abstain from
naming. The roster builder preserves person entitlements. Centroid naming keeps
conversation-resolution thresholds, evidence minimum and owner arbitration;
public provenance uses `speaker_label_provenance.project_source`.

A sufficiently covered AHC result containing exactly one evidenced voice skips
pyannote and uses the same assignment/naming stage. Missing/partial evidence never
qualifies for this shortcut. V1 comparisons remain in `transcription_shadow_results/v1`;
v2 scalar outcomes/comparisons are separate. No private text or vectors enter metrics.

`TRANSCRIPTION_FINAL_PASS_SYNC_ENABLED=false` independently gates sync/WAL cutover.
When enabled for the same cohort, sync reuses its matching/storage WAV download
and passes absolute provider word clocks through the same triage, diarizer and naming seam.
It returns the legacy first-word origin; like legacy segments, sync intake adds the
first word's offset back, so persisted segments use the VAD file's `started_at` origin
(shared with its stored audio) and two-decimal clocks.
Failure retains legacy matching; manual owner reservations remain authoritative.
Live matching enters through an adapter preserving its clip policy unchanged.

`TRANSCRIPTION_FINAL_PASS_AUTHORITATIVE=false` independently gates record replacement.
Promotion requires complete audio and a current completed, unlocked conversation
revision. A transaction rechecks rollout, deletion and revision, verifies segment
correspondence, and changes only speaker fields on stored segments. Stored text,
IDs, clocks, translations and structured source IDs remain unchanged. Manual
receipts are resolved to the retained IDs; person edits and either direction of a
bare legacy `is_user` disagreement are protected. Conflicting speakers within a
stored segment, unsafe mappings, translation materializations and app/note derived
references block promotion. The existing encryption boundary protects the write.
This background speaker update does not rerun downstream memory
or summary jobs; authoritative rollout must qualify that integration before enablement.

Deploy the additive diarizer image first, then a separate batch Helm release
(`batch_main:app`, `DIARIZATION_V2_ENABLED=true`) on its own GPUs, then backend
and pusher with all cutover flags off, then a separately reviewed cohort flag PR.
The default embedding/v1 app eagerly warms its models before TCP readiness and
keeps concurrent legacy routes without the batch lock. The batch app warms its
models before listening; failures abort startup. `/v2/diarization` is reached
only through the shared internal Gateway `/diarizer-batch` prefix. Cloud Run
cannot use ClusterIP DNS. Production batch runs on the dedicated tainted T4
`diarizer-batch-pool`, with a one-pod David-only floor; qualify the direct per-pod
Prometheus queue scrape and adapter rule before ramping. Exact install/metric/cohort gates: `scripts/diarization_eval/batch_install.md`. See `scripts/diarization_eval/capacity.md`
for routing and the opt-in release overlay; verify caller reachability before
cohort enablement.
Rollback: disable authoritative/sync flags first, set mode to `legacy`, then restore
previous backend and diarizer image digests if needed. No stored-history reversal
is automatic. Shared scorer instructions: `scripts/diarization_eval/README.md`.

### Flagged ladder usage admission

`LADDER_V1_BUDGET_ENFORCEMENT_ENABLED` (default OFF) lets the shared allowance
resolver use the monthly blended ledger for Free and ladder-price subscribers.
At 100% it selects the on-device allowance, never a captured-content lock. Raw
sync admission returns the structured at-cap choice before consuming device
WALs. `LADDER_V1_LANE_METERING_ENABLED` (default OFF) attributes hourly shadow
writes to live, batch, on-device or unknown lanes without double-charging the
feature total. See [ladder cutover](ladder-v1-cutover.md) for ramp prerequisites,
partial-coverage limits, the choice API and rollback.
### Flagged mobile on-device overflow

`mobile-on-device-overflow` is default off. Mobile phone-only WAL records
exclude audio from automatic and manual cloud drains; native batch files use
`omibatchoverflow` in the existing recordings directory. The runner decodes
bounded 30-second windows without diarization and submits transcript text to
`POST /v1/conversations/from-segments` with a stable `client_session_id`.
`stt_provider=on_device:<engine>` survives the canonical ingest seam. Identity
remains one unnamed voice; the engine token is provenance, not a speaker claim.
Optional Foundation Models summaries use the existing hash-bound
`client_processing` display projection (`runtime=local`). Unsupported devices
submit text for ordinary cloud processing; this path does not send audio to live
STT or charge live transcription hours. Phone-only mode submits nothing and
keeps transcript-only conversations in the account-owned local WAL index.
`/reprocess` processes the existing transcript and does not re-transcribe audio;
mobile therefore hides the cloud re-transcription action for these recordings.
### Mobile Standard batch admission (default off)

New Flutter clients reuse `client_state` visibility and add
`mobile_batch_capability_v1: true` to request authorization on the existing listen
control channel (renewed every 60 seconds). Listen's `service_status` events
`ready` and `mobile_batch_admission` carry the optional boolean
`mobile_standard_batch`; absent/false means normal streaming. Permission expires
on the client after two minutes without renewal and on every socket close.
`MOBILE_STANDARD_BATCH_ENABLED` and `MOBILE_STANDARD_BATCH_PERCENT` default to
off/0; the private Firestore `mobile-standard-batch` cohort selects explicit
members. `MOBILE_STANDARD_BATCH_UIDS` remains a migration fallback only. Admission also requires
private-cloud sync, ordinary STT and no enabled integrations or developer audio/
realtime-transcript webhook. Inventory reads are limited to once per 30 seconds;
unknown inventory keeps streaming. Transcript visibility and voice commands
win per packet on the phone.

Batch-owned pendant frames never enter the socket or streaming safety WAL. Every
original Opus frame, including silence and padding, is journaled using the
existing WAL framing and sealed files enter normal `/v2/sync-local-files` recovery.
The sync upload path archives originals under `raw-mobile-batch/{uid}/` in the
private-cloud bucket before acceptance or decoding. Enhanced protection encrypts
unchanged bytes; the account-deletion storage gate/purge owns this prefix. It has
no application TTL. Sync playback and backend VAD's conversation assignment stay
unchanged. Deploy archive acceptance on backend-sync before advertising permission
on listen; rollback revokes new batching but continues accepting pending files.
Production admission binds `MOBILE_STANDARD_BATCH_ENABLED=true` and
`MOBILE_STANDARD_BATCH_PERCENT=0` only on backend-listen. Seed intended members
in `rollout_cohorts/{uid}.cohorts.prod.mobile-standard-batch` with the
[operator script](runbooks/rollout-cohorts.md); keep the UID env key absent.
Dev membership uses its separate `cohorts.dev` map in the same customer project.
Sync archive acceptance is unconditional; backend, sync workers and pusher
require no admission flags. Demand-driven STT presence must finish its global
presence rollout and drain old sockets before enabling the mobile cohort.
Production Helm
workflow source is pinned to `main`; its branch input cannot select an
intermediate presence-only commit. The lead must pre-roll presence on the
existing dark revision before dispatching the cohort workflow.
Policy, storage-parity evidence, bounds, VAD benchmark and lead-owned 5%/12-hour
ramp: [mobile Standard batch](../../app/docs/mobile-standard-batch.md).

### Forward canonical audio archive (default off)

`AUDIO_CANONICAL_PACKING_ENABLED=true` plus `BUCKET_CANONICAL_AUDIO` routes new
mobile as-captured Opus to a separate regional canonical archive. Pusher also
archives its supplied PCM16 capture bytes there after committing playback. Both
intakes retain their existing primary writes first; optional canonical errors
are logged and metered without failing those writes. Mobile keeps writing its
`raw-mobile-batch/` object during rollout. Successful bucket policy reads are
cached for five minutes per client and bucket. No existing objects are packed. Owner/day tar shards carry stored-byte
SHA-256, source generations, formats, provenance and conversation ranges. Source
erasure requires hash verification and embedded-manifest round-trip; durable
owner intents resume partial cleanup. Account wipe sweeps sources, packs and
indexes even when the flag is off. Linked conversation audio deletion rewrites
only affected packs. Bucket provisioning, exact lifecycle rules, pricing and
rollback are in `deploy/storage-lifecycle/FORWARD.md`; this code does not apply
bucket policies or deploy a scheduler.

Canonical conversation erasure has a durable owner-scoped deletion marker.
Intake checks it before and after source/receipt publication, cleans racing
uploads, and packing rejects marked captures. Manual merges preserve canonical
bytes, retarget packed and unpacked conversation ranges to the survivor, and
publish transitive source→survivor links for late retries. Manifest rewrites are
durable and verified; later survivor deletion re-lists sources, indexes and packs
before releasing the owner gate. Only the explicit privacy endpoint and account
wipe erase canonical audio. Shared cache/merge cleanup never implies privacy erasure.
PCM ranges include sample counts, full tail duration and plaintext byte ranges;
enhanced encryption envelopes use a **big-endian** uint32 length prefix.

### Historical sync queue retirement

Redis sync jobs retain their existing 24-hour TTL by default. Two optional
write-time settings accept integer seconds from 3600 through 86400; unset,
invalid, or out-of-range values preserve 86400. `SYNC_JOB_COMPLETED_TTL_SECONDS`
changes only the TTL atomically published with a winning `completed` mutation
(both legacy and run-token-fenced CAS). Queued, processing, failed and partial
failure records, pending indexes, retry checkpoints and run leases keep their
existing lifetimes. A shorter successful-result window means a client returning
later may see 404 and re-upload its retained WAL; existing content/segment
deduplication still applies, but reconciliation traffic can increase. Failed
results retain 24 hours for dead-letter and retry-material cleanup.

`SYNC_JOB_METRIC_ONCE_TTL_SECONDS` changes only `terminal_outcome_metric` and
`segment_outcome:*` guards. A retry after this window can count telemetry again;
`speech_ms`, `dg_ms`, `usage` and unknown tags retain 24 hours. Do not shorten
billing guards based only on nominal retry count: queue pauses, dispatch delay
and long-running attempts also bound their lifetime. Neither setting rewrites
existing Redis entries or deletes results on completion. Rollback restores the
default for future writes; already-shortened entries cannot be recovered.

Backfill ownership and expired-head retirement are defined in [the two-lane runbook](runbooks/sync-two-lane.md#expired-head-retirement-train-1). Under `SYNC_BACKFILL_RETIRE_EXPIRED_AT_CLAIM`, Redis-absent unbound waiting work is retired transactionally before dispatch, with a bounded loop and a per-job run lock. Dispatching owners are reconciled under the same guard; running/bound work remains protected. Retirement emits `sync_backfill_expired_retired` and never counts as completed transcription or deletes client audio. Flutter/macOS retain queued WAL audio for completion or 404-triggered re-upload.

Backfill admission transactionally creates or repairs its sequencer owner. Completion and retirement transactionally park empty owners outside the hot reconcile query while retaining their fencing epochs; admission wakes them atomically. The bounded pending reconcile scan self-heals legacy missing owners over a configurable window (default 30 minutes), honors the staggered deadline before claiming or retiring waiting work, and meters `sync_backfill_owner_repaired`. The operator-only dry-run/apply procedure is in [backfill owner repair](runbooks/sync-two-lane.md#backfill-owner-repair).

### Bounded backfill preparation

The per-owner sequencer can warm a bounded prefix of staged-audio downloads while its head runs. Execution remains serial, including adjacent recordings, assignment, merge and billing. `SYNC_BACKFILL_OWNER_CONCURRENCY_ENABLED=off` stops preparation and ignores cached bytes; the default width is 2 only with at least 8 pending jobs. Generation validation protects deletion and replacement. Queue limits and backfill maxScale are unchanged. Bounds, fairness and throughput limits are in [bounded owner preparation](runbooks/sync-owner-concurrency.md).

### Sync VAD segment cache

`vad_is_empty(..., cache=True)` stores sync VAD segments in Redis for 24 hours by
default. `VAD_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` is read when each cache entry is written and
accepts integer seconds from 300 through 86400; unset, invalid, or out-of-range
values keep the 86400-second default. It is declared at 86400 for the backend,
sync, and sync-backfill Cloud Run services in both dev and prod. A cache miss
recomputes VAD, so a shorter setting reduces cache residency while increasing
repeat VAD work. Existing entries keep their original expiry.

The default-oldest `SYNC_BACKFILL_ORDER` policy can select descending pending
capture time for a UID cohort, including persisted work draining while sequencer
admission is off. Direct tasks receive a bounded newer-first eligibility
preference; Cloud Tasks does not guarantee order among eligible tasks. Segment
assignment remains chronological within a job, and atomic interval intake
merges late earlier chunks across jobs. The existing pending sweep retires
Redis-expired old tails without modifying a newer active owner, audio or user
data. Index prerequisites, cohort controls and limitations are in the
[newest-first rollout](runbooks/sync-two-lane.md#newest-first-rollout).

### Shared Gateway caller routes

NLLB, VAD and every speaker-embedding host (including Parakeet fallback) use
the environment-specific shared Gateway paths. Parakeet STT retains its legacy
hostname. Dev uses `172.16.48.49`; prod uses `172.16.160.20`. Qualify Gateway
programming and caller reachability before deploying.

Caller deploy order, route requirements and independent rollback are documented
in [the Gateway caller runbook](internal-gateway-callers.md).

### Sync batch final-pass admission

The optional sync final pass shares a fleet Redis budget across fresh and backfill
(10 audio-hours and 1,000 attempts per UTC hour) and one in-flight call per process.
Admission refuses immediately, before embeddings and batch work. Redis connect/read
I/O has finite 0.5s timeouts; a stalled peer cannot retain the final-pass slot. Exhaustion, Redis
failure or invalid limits retains legacy speakers and logs
`event=sync_final_pass outcome=skipped_budget`. Retries consume another reservation.
`TRANSCRIPTION_SYNC_FINAL_PASS_HOURLY_AUDIO_HOURS` and
`TRANSCRIPTION_SYNC_FINAL_PASS_HOURLY_REQUESTS` declare the limits. Production defaults stay at stage B (David-only); selecting complete
stage C explicitly enables 1% plus David on all four hosts. Outcome/failure logs use the full
SHA-256 of `sync-final-pass:` plus UID for correlation, with no identity metric label.

Long-audio batch qualification uses the same final-pass HTTP client and public
corpus only; see [the stage C load method and probe commands](diarizer-batch-stagec-load.md).
Its wall/RTF scenarios remain unqualified until the lead fills the timing table.

### As-captured archive

Per David's 2026-10-22 ruling (P71), the as-captured archive is a fleet
default, not a trial: `AUDIO_ARCHIVE_LIVE_ENABLED` and
`AUDIO_ARCHIVE_READ_ENABLED` default **on** (flag-only kill switches, no
percent cohort) so the original live Opus packets are captured before they are
transformed for STT/pusher, using the same `omi-wal-uint32le-opus-v1` container
and shared canonical writer as the mobile Standard archive. Live packs are
owner/conversation/hour/channel bounded at 16 MiB; non-Opus PCM uses lossless
FLAC. v1/v2 offline intake archives original uploaded files before decode and
acceptance (still behind `AUDIO_ARCHIVE_SYNC_ENABLED`). Raw metadata records
unknown legacy provenance explicitly; storage consent never authorizes training.

PCM remains available in dual-write. READ prefers complete, checksum-verified
raw spans; STOP_PCM (default off, ramps on `AUDIO_ARCHIVE_STOP_PCM_PERCENT`,
never the shared `AUDIO_ARCHIVE_PERCENT` that COALESCE pins at 100) skips a
batch only when committed raw reconstructs every PCM range, verified by
capture-PCM hash or byte-identical decode. Missing spans,
checksum failures or identity mismatches keep today's
path. Rollback STOP_PCM first; retain READ for raw-only history until verified
PCM regeneration. Archive tail flushing runs after STT drain, outside its deadline.
Regional cache routing never relocates canonical capture. Account and conversation
deletion purge live and noncurrent generations across configured buckets.

The [archive runbook](runbooks/sync-ingest-archive.md) owns flag phases, bucket
provisioning, C023 estimates, playback/byte/deletion canaries and the STOP_PCM
≤5%/≥12h ramp.
### Recent LEGACY offline-sync admission

V2 resolves identity from `X-App-Platform` + `X-Device-Id-Hash`, but grants
DEVICE_BOUND capture-time trust only after verifying `X-Omi-Sync-Capture-Manifest`
and the server conversation window. A present device header alone is not proof.
Pure offline capture has no such server window; partial conversation drains
cannot claim the existing immutable manifest either.

`SYNC_LEGACY_FRESH_ENABLED=true` (code/dev/prod default false) lets whole LEGACY
batches whose oldest capture is within `SYNC_FRESH_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` enter fresh
capacity only for private `sync-legacy-fresh` cohort members (the old UID env
list is a migration fallback), or a stable SHA-256 UID bucket in
`SYNC_LEGACY_FRESH_PERCENT` (default
zero). Buckets match transcription final pass; the master flag disables both.
Malformed/non-finite percentages select nobody beyond the allowlist; finite
percentages clamp to 0–100. The atomic Redis token reservation still applies to
every selected account. Existing historical jobs are never moved. Trust remains LEGACY. Each
request costs `max(1, ceil(actual multipart bytes / MiB))`; defaults refill 60
tokens/hour with a ten-token burst per account across replicas and devices.
`SYNC_LEGACY_FRESH_HOURLY_TOKENS` supports 1–120 and
`SYNC_LEGACY_FRESH_BURST_TOKENS` 1–60. Bad config, unknown file size or Redis
failure demotes to backfill. Budget Redis connect/read I/O has a one-second
timeout and runs off the event loop. Upload retries spend again; timestamp/session-id
changes cannot reset the account budget. The budget bounds work admission,
not billed speech. A fresh-only hard restriction or daily ceiling demotes a
promoted LEGACY batch to backfill before persistence and final admission metrics,
so optional promotion never introduces a fair-use 429. DEVICE_BOUND fresh keeps
its existing 429 response. Trusted manifests retain their existing route. Future,
invalid/missing timestamps, mixed historical batches and lookback rejection
keep their existing semantics. Deprecated V1 stays unchanged.

`omi_sync_lane_admissions_total{lane,trust,reason}` and `sync_lane_admission`
record the final budget and fresh-gate decision. New reasons: `recent_legacy_capture`,
`legacy_fresh_budget_exceeded`, `legacy_fresh_budget_unavailable`,
`legacy_fresh_hard_restricted`, `legacy_fresh_daily_ceiling`. No account
labels are added. Optional final-pass logs now include `lane` and
`duration_seconds`, covering decoding, embedding triage, diarization and naming
inside the adapter. These times exclude the preceding WAV download and are
not whole-job latency. See [drain qualification](runbooks/sync-legacy-fresh-drain.md).

### Sync assignment contention retry

Fresh uploads can overlap for one account: clients serialize the HTTP POST, but
continue uploading after the asynchronous 202 response. Backfill sequencing
reduces overlap among backfill jobs; it does not exclude fresh/live writes.
Atomic assignment reads/writes `users/{uid}/sync_assignment/recent`, relevant
UTC day index documents, and canonical/donor conversation documents. Segments
are embedded in conversations; separate content ledgers do not serialize these
assignment transactions.

`SYNC_ASSIGNMENT_CONTENTION_RETRY_ENABLED=true` (default false everywhere) wraps
sync assignment in the existing shared bounded contention helper. Five outer
attempts use fresh transactions and equal-jitter backoff (maximum 2.4s total
sleep), retaining the SDK's five commit attempts and the existing three-attempt
expired-ID restart. The outer budget also handles read-time `Aborted`, which the
SDK's commit-only retry misses. Only explicit aborts/cause chains are replayed;
ambiguous transport failures and unrelated invalid arguments still escape.
Indexes, conversation append, dedupe and revision ownership remain atomic.
The static `firestore_transaction_contention operation=sync_assignment` log
records retry/recovered/exhausted without identity/content fields. Disable the
flag to restore the previous boundary; no schema migration is needed.

A later content revision may fence an older enrichment pass after its intake
already committed. That job can ACK `outcome=superseded` with zero successful
segments. Prove convergence with processed-segment receipts and actual segment
IDs in the surviving conversation; a zero-success ACK alone is not proof of
no loss. User-deletion/assignment supersession is a different path and must not
be treated as evidence that content landed. Qualification requires every
synthetic uploaded segment to appear exactly once, including these cases.

Sync's trailing-edge `sync_reprocess_outbox` records the enrichment obligation
before dispatch, including the batch projection's atomic ready receipt. A host
without a complete queue, sync worker handler, matching OIDC audience, and
invoker identity persists `dispatch=null`; it does not retry an invalid binding.
Production backend-integration uses the fresh `sync-jobs` binding from the runtime
manifest. The projection pump and backend-sync's two-minute sequencer sweep both
repair due obligations. Repair can bind an incomplete legacy row inside its CAS,
rotating the task token while retaining version, quiet-window, and lease fences.
Valid backfill bindings remain on their original worker and queue. Unbound hosts
skip invalid rows and increment `sync_reprocess_unbound_total{action="repair"}`
instead of emitting a per-row repair error.

### First-summary deadline and repair (dark)

`FIRST_SUMMARY_DEADLINE_ENABLED` (default false) caps the existing outbox's first
summary dispatch at original pending time + `FIRST_SUMMARY_DEADLINE_SECONDS`
(default 120, bounded 60–120); the private `first-summary` cohort supports a
canary. A nonempty old UID env list remains a migration fallback; both empty
and absent keys deny nonmembers unless the independent percentage ramp admits
them, preserving main #22113.
First-summary work bypasses demand-tier waiting and uses a 120s lease, renewed
every 30s for the same owner/task token while the request is alive. Only a
replacement conversation owner fences persistence; the same owner's result can
commit after its original expiry. The claim uses the already-loaded snapshot;
each heartbeat adds a CAS read/write. Three empty kept results terminally block
the obligation with `empty_output_exhausted`, outside the indexed due scan,
without clearing any structured content. Intake discards never enter that index.
Appends and retries preserve the original time; later enriched revisions retain coalescing.

`FIRST_SUMMARY_PRESERVE_ADMITTED_ENABLED=false` is an independent dark drain
control on integration and both sync task handlers. With the deadline master switch
still on, an existing finite `summary_deadline_at` keeps its dispatch ceiling,
demand bypass, short lease/heartbeat, repair cadence and output requirement after
percent/allowlist rollback. A scoped owner context carries that policy through the
synchronous processing/persistence callback and resets on every exit. New outbox
marks ignore that context, so nested marks cannot widen cohort admission. A callback
returning no output retains the admitted obligation without consuming the existing
three-empty-model-result budget. A confirmed persisted rule discard settles normally. Completed/effects-only rows and new out-of-cohort conversations
retain their existing coalescing behavior. No read, new queue or paid-intent reset
is added. It cannot reconstruct already-deleted outboxes; existing debt repair
requires its own explicit enablement and eligible owner cohort. Turning the
preserve control off restores legacy rollout behavior; turning the deadline master
off disables deadline handling entirely.

`SUMMARY_DEBT_REPAIR_ENABLED` (default false) adds completed server-minimum debt
to the existing repair owner: one globally leased two-minute page, at most 100
rows and ten admission attempts. It reuses the same outbox/generation, checks
manual/discard/deletion/privacy/local-only/BYOK/lease fences, and emits terminal
summary receipts only after the fenced write. No new claim reads. A rendered
section body or nonempty overview is required; a title/actions/events alone do
not settle debt. The bounded debt gauges include their sample timestamp.

[Index readiness, read budget, historical-provenance limits and enable/rollback](runbooks/summary-guarantee.md)
are part of the rollout contract. This PR ships dark and does not bulk-repair
unmarked historical rows or change record-projection completion semantics.

### Dormant batch pull protocol

[Batch pipeline worker](batch-pipeline-worker.md) defines a separate default-off
pull queue with 64 owner-hash scheduler shards and per-owner ordered result log.
The sync route selects it only with `BATCH_PIPELINE_ENABLED` plus owner
allowlist/percentage; an atomic content-ledger protocol seal prevents a legacy
twin. Admission rollback leaves accepted work draining with 20s heartbeats.
Compute failures retain the ordered head and retry with a 600s backoff cap;
historical dead heads can be redriven by authenticated internal RPC or owner
polling. Privacy-fenced recovery is isolated without clearing deletion fences.
Model VAD ends are cropped to the actual sample buffer, and ASR window clocks
use the integer slice origin. When detected speech has no owned ASR words,
the Parakeet worker retries once on the entire capture, as a single-item batch
with 2s of synthetic silence on each edge (at least 8s total). This removes VAD
cuts and core ownership from the retry; clocks are rebased to the original
capture and synthetic-padding words are excluded. The padded retry must fit
both 120s and the configured padded budget; larger units are retained whole
for fenced legacy recovery, never truncated. Remaining empty ASR is an
observable failure, never silence; safe reason/count diagnostics are specified
in the [worker runtime](../diarizer/batch_pipeline_runtime.md).
The scheduler quarantines exhausted failures while preserving their audio and
content claim. The default-off, owner-allowlisted fenced legacy recovery path
is defined in [serving settings](batch-pipeline-serving.md); the worker retry
does not dispatch legacy STT or change its enablement.
Late captures receive 202, retain their original clock and append after the
committed tail; no unsupported 409 or capture-watermark regression occurs.
Conversation projection, deletion, indexes READY and GPU qualification remain
rollout gates. See [serving settings](batch-pipeline-serving.md).

### Dark batch-worker GPU and compaction boundaries (PR 21674)

Conversation-sized compute belongs on dedicated batch GPUs. Shared-live compute
must use the existing live model arbiter, units and total pulls <=120 audio
seconds, and one in-flight batch per GPU; dispatch priority cannot preempt a
running inference. `BATCH_PIPELINE_POOL_KIND` and `BATCH_PIPELINE_UNIT_SECONDS`
bound worker units independently of durable conversation identity. The local
multi-model worker factory rejects live co-location.

Default-off `TRANSCRIPTION_FINAL_PASS_VAD_COMPACTION_ENABLED` runs local speech
VAD before stored-audio shadow ASR and shared pyannote final-pass inference.
300 ms onset/500 ms tail padding and exact sample maps restore original word,
turn and overlap clocks. VAD failure or ambiguous word mapping retains original
behavior; legacy/single-speaker resolution stays on original clocks. The sync
final-pass adapter receives existing words and does not add an STT request.
No Nemotron cascade is routed. WAL pre-STT proof matching is unchanged; bounded
coverage outcome/reason diagnostics now explain conservative abstention.

### Repeat-work cuts (plan v3, default off)

`PLAYBACK_ON_DEMAND_ENABLED=false` preserves eager playback warming. When enabled,
chunk-list refreshes persist metadata without warming MP3s; `/v1/sync/audio/{id}/urls`,
precache on open, and audio download enqueue the existing named audio-merge tasks.
GCS listing generations/checksums plus the complete ordered chunk geometry select
stable UUIDs. Entries without immutable source evidence retain fresh IDs. The worker
verifies stamped sources before/after merge. Conversation MP3s use immutable
fingerprint paths, including the conversation origin used for seek offsets. The
conversation key also includes `CONVERSATION_BUILD_FORMAT` (bump on gap-collapse,
span-projection or export changes), with a canonical audio-file list order.
Corrupt span sidecars invalidate only the observed MP3/sidecar generations and
rebuild as a cache miss; missing legacy sidecars are published for stamp-retry
reuse. See `docs/runbooks/playback-write-amplification.md` for force regeneration. Raw
capture bytes, metadata, retention and consent rules remain unchanged. Up to 128 recent prior IDs per group resolve to the current source identity (never
to an old artifact); stored objects remain untouched. Clients refresh `/urls` after
a longer revision history or a group split. Newly rebuilt groups receive stable IDs.
Demand tasks carry their source-identity protocol through rollback and use separate hourly task names so eager-task tombstones cannot swallow
first play; run locks coalesce cross-hour deliveries. The first request may return
pending. Measure `omi_playback_demand_to_ready_seconds` (queue/retry included),
`omi_playback_build_seconds`, and `omi_playback_artifacts_total` before widening.
Before acknowledging a pre-enable eager task, write an opaque playback recovery
epoch to the existing conversation, without reading or recreating it. A failed
write returns 503. On rollback, open/play readers reuse their loaded conversation
to select a fresh, deduped eager task name, escaping the old task's tombstone.
This adds one metadata write per deferred delivery, without a recurring queue
wake or a new Firestore read. Track `omi_playback_artifacts_total{outcome="deferred"}`.
Flag-off readers also reuse a matching immutable demand artifact and its spans.

`REPEAT_WORK_REVISION_ENABLED=false` preserves Train 1 controls. Enabled uses the
same #21679 `sync_reprocess_outbox`, leases, repair sweep and explicit Process Now
bypass. Quiet-window extension is capped at 60 minutes from the first pending mark;
new revisions during execution remain owed with their original first-pending clock.
The step-1 demand scheduler owns surface
promotion and the 1/5/60-minute tiers; this flag introduces no second queue or surface
inference. Notes (including task generation) and L1 reuse only exact effective inputs
with successful durable checkpoints, using the already loaded conversation snapshot
for cache checks. Fingerprints include source revision, rendered transcript/context,
language, owner evidence, prompt/schema implementation, resolved route and
`REPEAT_WORK_LLM_VERSION` (default `2026-10-16-v1`). Bump that version in the same
config train as a remote gateway model/prompt change. A failed output never stamps.
Task identity and export preservation remain owned by `action_item_refresh.py`.

`omi_repeat_work_features_total` counts invoked/reused/durable feature outcomes;
`omi_repeat_work_llm_calls_total` counts observed LangChain completions/errors within
the conversation processing scope. Content-free `event=repeat_work` logs join these
to durable finalizations by hashed owner/conversation key and source revision; IDs
never become metric labels. Group by the final surviving key, not finalization-event
count. This covers observed logical calls, not hidden provider transport retries or
external Jev calls; reconcile the attempt ledger/invoices before booking dollars.

`SYNC_POLL_BACKOFF_ENABLED=false` preserves existing responses. Enabled GET
`/v2/sync-local-files/{job_id}` returns `poll_after_ms=60000` for queued work,
`20000` for active/unknown work or `?surface_open=true`, and `0` for terminal work
(stop polling). It uses already loaded job state, without an account-wide Firestore
query; a terminal job is not proof all other jobs are idle. Both Redis and durable
batch status responses use the same hint. The Flutter generated response parser and
`SyncJobPoller.fetch` honor it with a 20s minimum; terminal policy stops polls. Existing
clients do not send the optional surface hint, so queued foreground work retains the
60s hint until it becomes active; request-to-active freshness is a rollout guard.

Rollback each new flag independently. Existing durable enrichment obligations drain
with their original queue/audience after rollback. Do not change Train 1 production
switches as part of the rollback. This PR changes no production runtime configuration.

### Mobile ring-download idle recovery

The phone's ring-storage reader retains its initial five-second response limit
and 30-minute overall limit, and now ends an incomplete transfer after 15
seconds without further DATA following BEGIN/data. Radio arrival resets the
idle window before queued custody/disk work; control chatter does not. STOP
uses the original connection epoch, accepted notifications drain, and complete
records enter the existing durable phone WAL pipeline. The source remains
pending and no unproven frontier advance or processing success is invented.
This neither changes cloud job dispatch nor makes legacy firmware's early
reclaim durable. Scope and acceptance checklist:
`app/docs/offline-sync-stall-recovery.md`.

### Demand ramp telemetry

Listen pins `demand_cohort=admitted|eligible_control|ineligible` once before
initial provider selection, using the demand allocation and existing safety
predicates. Eligible controls include flag-off sessions; ineligible includes
BYOK, multi-channel, custom STT, onboarding, phone calls, recovery-off and
unqualified language shapes. Visibility, handovers, recovery and kill-switch
observations never reassign this intent-to-treat cohort.

The transcript-outcome, recovery-attempt, configured-chain-exhaustion,
terminal-after-text, window-first-text and serving-lane metrics retain their
names and gain that label. Aggregate queries must sum across it; histogram
queries retain `le`. Existing monitoring definitions already aggregate these
series. Completed-session exhaustion is separately exported as
`omi_listen_demand_exhausted_sessions_total{demand_cohort,outcome="yes|no"}`;
this counts sockets once, whereas chain exhaustion and fallback detail count
hops. `omi_listen_demand_failure_detail_total` exposes bounded
`first_text_deadline|capacity_full|replay_ring_overflow|gate_unavailable|other` reasons and capacity subtypes without
changing the shared `omi_fallback_total` schema.

`omi_stt_provider_wire_audio_seconds_total{provider,demand_cohort}` counts PCM16
seconds **after successful transport writes**, once per actual write including
replay. Soniox and Modulate observe their async websocket writers; Modulate
excludes a prepended WAV header. Deepgram observes the synchronous websocket
below the SDK's exception-to-success conversion. Failed writes, JSON controls,
keepalives and EOS do not count. These are successful local transport writes,
not vendor acknowledgements or invoices. Only listen-created transports count.
Pair wire seconds with `omi_listen_demand_capture_audio_seconds_total` (decoded
mono capture before VAD/replay) and `omi_listen_live_lane_seconds_total` (adopted
serving wall time, including silence). Capture increments during ingestion;
controls flush lane seconds at teardown, admitted demand sessions also flush
periodically. Use drained controls for a complete lane-time comparison.

`omi_listen_demand_output_seconds{demand_cohort,trigger}` measures first decoded
audio to delivered text (`first_output`) and a valid hidden-to-visible report
to the next delivered text (`visible_return`). Repeated visible heartbeats do
not reset the wait. Invalid reports preserve the last valid telemetry visibility
and its pending wait; routing still treats invalid state as unknown. It includes
provider/replay delay and time without speech;
compare matched exposure. `omi_listen_demand_output_events_total` counts
`started|delivered|no_output` waits so censored latencies cannot look healthy.
A wait ends without output at teardown or when the client leaves visibility.
No UIDs, session identifiers, text, audio or unbounded provider names enter
these metrics. Telemetry changes neither admission percentages nor policy.

Demand telemetry is optional at every session boundary. Bootstrap pins the
admission decision independently of instrumentation and does not access the
receiver before `_build_components`. Receiver construction applies the cohort
to its optional lane meter. Bootstrap-only runtime construction initializes
telemetry and recovery pins without requiring `__init__`. Missing or faulty
telemetry resolves to `ineligible`; failed label binding, counter/histogram
updates, lane observations and wire observations cannot interrupt bootstrap,
audio writes, transcript delivery or teardown. Missing measurements remain a
ramp HOLD, never evidence of healthy exposure.

### Dark record correctness controls

`BATCH_PIPELINE_AUTHORITATIVE_LABELS_ENABLED=false` preserves today's projection.
When enabled, successful batch pyannote turns persist `final:batch:<unit>` speaker
scopes, shared across that unit's projection parts. These scopes survive rollback;
enrichment skips conversation-wide AHC when any such segment is present, including
mixed live/WAL conversations. There are no enrollment-compatible worker centroids:
identities stay anonymous, owner-safe, and gaps carry explicit `batch_abstention`.
Existing manual receipts still apply; text, translations and segment IDs are retained.
Recovery output keeps legacy scopes and never claims pyannote authority.

`BATCH_PIPELINE_REQUIRE_LIVE_TARGET_ENABLED=false` is the default. When enabled,
projection with an explicit target waits if that row has not persisted; replay uses
the same transaction and receipt. This closes the WAL-before-live-create race
without adding a target lookup or weakening source/device/privacy checks. Captures
without an explicit target retain existing lineage and assignment policies.

`BATCH_PIPELINE_EMPTY_ASR_RECOVERY_ENABLED=false` preserves bounded GPU retries.
When enabled on the worker, nonempty VAD plus empty ASR after the padded retry
hands the unit directly to existing quarantine recovery. Empty ASR is never silence.
`BATCH_PIPELINE_RECOVERY_MODE=on` and the existing recovery UID allowlist run David's
recovery. `BATCH_PIPELINE_RECOVERY_ALL_ADMITTED=false` additionally permits the same
bounded sweep across already-admitted owners when enabled, replacing the owner
filter on the existing query. A bounded process-local document-ID cursor advances
past held/capped/running rows and wraps on an empty page, without a second query
or document lookup. The durable prepared/running token forbids repeated
paid STT after timeout, lost reply or process death. `failed_held` needs separate
operator disposition; no automatic reset or second paid attempt is permitted.

`BATCH_PIPELINE_SPEECH_EMPTY_MULTILINGUAL_ENABLED=false` keeps the existing CPU
recovery selector. When enabled, strict-VAD-positive empty units use Modulate
once within their paid intent, avoiding another Parakeet call for unset intake
language. Existing strict-VAD silence evidence remains required for silence.
The separate historical operator is dry-run default, owner/census/digest-bound
and capped at 67 units in an explicitly approved paid epoch 2; it preserves epoch
1 and only reconciles running calls. Valid results use normal sibling release
and upload completion. See [recovery and rollback](batch-pipeline-serving.md).

`BATCH_PIPELINE_CORRECTNESS_METRICS_ENABLED=false` enables no counters by default.
When enabled, existing committed transitions emit
`omi_batch_pipeline_correctness_transitions_total{event,source}` and
`omi_batch_pipeline_correctness_audio_seconds_total{event}`. Events are admitted,
failed (terminal quarantine, once per unit), recovered (recovery settlement),
committed, projection_completed (one durable upload completion) and
empty_asr_attempt (raw GPU failures, may repeat). Commit sources distinguish
`gpu_pump` from CPU `ordered_merge`; aggregate across both processes.
`omi_batch_pipeline_projected_segments_total{authority=batch|legacy|abstained}`
counts newly persisted survivors, not input words or complete conversations.
No telemetry reads are added. Counters can miss a lost commit reply or process
exit; they do not replace durable receipts. Projection completion establishes
canonical persistence and enrichment scheduling, not completed summary/client display.

Enable plan for the authorized operator: deploy dark; enable these controls for
David with admission enabled, percent 0 and David on both admission and recovery
allowlists. Require at least 30 minutes of clean, nonempty correctness sentinel
observations. Set admission percent to 5 on every intake host and enable
`BATCH_PIPELINE_RECOVERY_ALL_ADMITTED=true` on integration; after another clean
30-minute sentinel, go directly to percent 100. Preserve the David allowlists.
Worker label/retry flags apply only to already-admitted work. Warm capacity and
budget checks precede each percentage flip. Close admission everywhere to roll
back, retaining worker, integration and recovery drain paths. Unsetting producer
flags does not remove persisted label authority or reset paid recovery intents.

### Batch projection contention and replay

`BATCH_PIPELINE_READ_ONLY_METRICS_ENABLED=false` is a dark control on integration.
When it and `BATCH_PIPELINE_SERVING_ENABLED=true` are enabled, the authenticated
batch metrics route only refreshes queue gauges on the database executor. It no
longer runs cleanup, enrichment repair, lease recovery, ordered merge or projection
on each scrape. The supervised serving loop and explicit reconcile RPC retain those
obligations, including after admission rollback. Without the serving switch, the
metrics route retains its legacy drain so this control cannot silently remove the
only configured drain. Replica/revision overlap still requires Firestore fences and
owner claims; this control does not serialize the remaining reconcilers.

`BATCH_PIPELINE_PROJECTION_TIMING_ENABLED=false` independently enables static,
content-free `event=batch_projection_phase` start/finish/error logs around read,
assignment, bridge, private playback, enrichment scheduling, accounting, completion
and publication. Durations use the monotonic clock. No owner, segment, conversation,
payload, or exception message is logged. A started phase without its terminal event
helps locate a stalled RPC or process shutdown; it is not a completed latency sample.
Keep first-summary eligibility and repair enabled for admitted owners while rolling
back batch admission: lowering `FIRST_SUMMARY_DEADLINE_PERCENT` also removes their
short dispatch/repair/lease policy, even when durable summary debt already exists.
These controls do not bypass predecessor ordering or the all-sibling completion fence.

`BATCH_PIPELINE_PROJECTION_CONTENTION_ENABLED=false` is the dark default on every serving role.
Enable it on integration to coalesce concurrent projection per owner using an advisory Redis
NX/300-second token lease with one-second connect/read timeouts. Token-checked release cannot remove a replacement lease; expiry
or Redis unavailability can allow overlap, so Firestore privacy, predecessor ordering and
assignment receipts remain authoritative. No Firestore lookup is added. Both projection and
batch assignment then use the existing bounded five-attempt fresh-transaction contention
retry with jitter, retaining the existing expired-transaction restart. Legacy assignment retains its separate `SYNC_ASSIGNMENT_CONTENTION_RETRY_ENABLED`
control. No STT or admission path is changed.

With the control enabled, silence persistence can discover another projector's receipt after its initial check. Resume
all post-persist obligations and durable completion; a replay alone is never client completion.
With the contention control on, content-free `action=project outcome=completed|waiting` and
`action=claim outcome=busy|unavailable` logs plus per-sweep completed/waiting/blocked/error summaries distinguish drain progress from contention. Keep
admission paused until accepted-work completion, visible synthetic canary and at least 30 minutes
