of correctness sentinels pass. Scaling all integration traffic to one instance is a temporary
capacity tradeoff, not a distributed projection fence (revision overlap remains possible).

## Durable live and desktop record admission (dark)

`BATCH_PIPELINE_LIVE_ADMISSION_ENABLED=false` is the sole new producer control.
When true, retained server-STT captures use the existing
`BATCH_PIPELINE_ENABLED` master switch, `BATCH_PIPELINE_PERCENT` hash ramp and
private Firestore `batch-pipeline` cohort. New in-progress rows
reserve `record_lane_owner=batch_live_v1` in their existing create write. Captures
already in progress at enablement reserve the same owner from the finalizer's
already-loaded revision using a conditional write, with zero extra reads on a
successful first claim. Failed claims reload and retry
up to three times inside the finalizer attempt. Exhausted contention queues durable
reconciliation without increasing the processing-failure count. HTTP acknowledges
the current delivery; pusher reports the existing queued response.
With admission enabled, the legacy final pass claims its owner from its loaded
revision before compute. Failed claims reload/retry up to three times, with up to
three scheduled rounds; only a stored batch owner transfers responsibility to the
record lane. Persistent contention fails explicitly at that bound. When dark,
an unowned legacy pass uses its existing read with no claim or refresh.
Deploy dark and drain pre-protocol legacy work before any future enablement. BYOK,
E2EE, missing retention consent and unsupported/unspecified multilingual routes
keep their existing supported processing path. The closed Parakeet language
vocabulary is shared with the STT policy; this switch never changes the live
provider wire.

Finalization registers a `record_pending` job before completing its durable job.
The pusher flushes the final buffered audio on finalize, conversation switch or
disconnect, then writes a blind `record_audio_closed` receipt only after every
queued upload succeeds. Queue/buffer drops and exhausted uploads never certify
completeness. CPU reconciliation waits for both that receipt and the completed,
retained conversation. This is a durability barrier, not an upload-grace timer.

The coordinator lists the already retained audio, verifies overlaps, and freezes
one-hour capture units as encrypted **reference manifests**, not copies of the
recording. Each owner/conversation-scoped raw reference pins its GCS generation,
PCM digest and exact sample slice. The worker authenticates/decrypts existing raw
objects and materializes the ordinary WAV contract. The existing artifact eraser
owns the temporary manifest only; its encrypted reference proof stays through
the 45-day receipt window for first late WAL uploads (GPU results retain their
24-hour cleanup), and privacy erasure is immediate; raw capture retention/deletion remains with its
original owner. `record_pending` uses the existing state/available-at query shape
and existing privacy erasure machinery; pending obligations have no TTL.

When live admission is enabled, named safety WALs and recording-session WALs
resolve the registered record before provider dispatch. An eligible in-progress
capture holds its WAL until finalization reserves the native owner. Another WAL
freezes its batch-sync or legacy dispatcher with a conditional owner write.
With the flag off, unowned named uploads perform no ownership reads or writes;
they use today's sync/BYOK dispatcher. Owners returned by existing provenance or
privacy snapshots route to their persisted dispatcher without another lookup.
No flag-off ownership discovery is added. A BYOK WAL targeting a known native
owner returns terminal HTTP 409 `record_capture_byok_conflict`, without Retry-After.
An unowned BYOK upload in the batch cohort retains the existing intake hold:
HTTP 503 `sync_batch_byok_unavailable`, with no sync-job creation or ledger claim.
Eligible server-STT WALs return the native public job only after exact
PCM interval/byte coverage is proven against its pinned references. A successful
proof aliases the existing upload-content ledger to that public job, so retries
survive artifact expiry and producer rollback. Missing tails, clock/byte conflicts and pending capture uploads return retryable
503 with local audio retained. Privacy refusals return terminal 409. Neither
creates a legacy twin. Desktop uploads targeting an existing
from-segments row use the same projection contract and atomically reserve
`batch_sync_v1`; sync upload metering remains on the existing sync path. Native
live record projection does not charge the same live speech a second time.

Record projection is transactional against the original conversation ID. It waits
for finalize, replaces unannotated provisional segments within the capture unit,
and increments the existing sync transcript revision. That revision fences a
stale finalizer. Delayed full-transcript/socket writes yield after record
projection; field-scoped translation/manual mutations remain supported. Annotated
segments retain their IDs, edited text, translations and references through the
shared fail-closed speaker remap. Ambiguous remaps retain the annotated sentence and explicitly abstain from
acoustic attribution; the user's manual receipt still wins. Genuine VAD silence clears only unprotected
provisional speech; empty ASR with nonempty VAD retains #21981's recovery contract.
Persisted record ownership excludes both the old #21424 shadow/final pass and
automatic smart merge. Producer flag rollback never grants the old owner anew.

### Rollout and read envelope

**Dark only: no enablement in this PR.** Every declaration of
`BATCH_PIPELINE_LIVE_ADMISSION_ENABLED` remains `false`, including pusher's explicit
runtime/chart declaration. The production `assign_sync_conversation` Aborted
incident identified in review now has a separate contention fix inherited from
main. That source merge establishes neither production recovery nor a real
canonical mobile/Mac canary; both remain prerequisites to rollout authorization.
The future sequence remains David, then 5%, then 100% jointly with sync, using the
existing correctness gates and capacity model; this is not permission to start it.

Future activation requires CPU/listen/pusher/integration to agree on the new flag.
While enabled, pusher may seal any successfully uploaded retained recording,
including a capture not yet stamped by the in-flight finalizer. When dark, it seals
only owners observed through its existing audio-metadata update snapshot. An
unowned close adds no lookup, close write or early buffer flush. Failed/dropped
uploads never certify closure. Integration pending-record scans run only while
the new flag is on; there is no per-tick query to discover whether owners exist.
Already admitted ready GPU work continues through the existing serving path.

For rollback with pending registrations or first-WAL proofs to drain, close new
admission with the existing batch master/percentage controls and private
`batch-pipeline` cohort membership
while retaining the live flag and serving/recovery paths. Set the new flag false
after those obligations resolve. Flag-off drains already-loaded owners and existing
content aliases, but does not discover unknown owners. Do not roll back to an image
lacking the persisted-owner/reference-manifest protocol. Unsetting producer flags
never removes persisted label authority.

This table counts **additional** operations relative to the existing path.
Existing provenance/privacy and audio-metadata reads are reused.

| Path | Flag off, unowned: added reads / writes | Enabled or already-owned path |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Named sync ownership routing | 0 / 0 | Discovery: one conversation read, plus one session lookup if needed. Loaded owners skip discovery. New owner: one conditional field write. |
| Pusher close | 0 / 0 | One blind close write per successful owned or explicitly enabled retained close; existing metadata read supplies owner hints. |
| Legacy final pass | 0 / 0 | One conditional owner write if unowned; each lost CAS adds one refresh read before a bounded retry. |
| Integration pending scan | 0 / 0 | At 20-second ticks, nominally 4,320 queries/day/process plus returned documents; empty queries bill at least one read. |
| Record admission | Existing sync protocol only | Patch only `record_lane_owner` once if missing; matching owners add no conversation write. Never set the conversation body. |
| Finalizer ownership claim | 0 / 0 | Loaded revision reused; each lost CAS adds one refresh read. Persistent contention adds one queued-job transaction without increasing processing attempts. |

There are zero added reads per audio frame, provisional segment or pusher flush.
For **enabled** daily native registrations `N`, discovery WAL reads `W`, session
lookups `S`, scan documents `D`, held retries `R`, manifest fetches `F`, integration
processes `I`, extra CAS snapshot reads `C` (including retried shadow rounds) and
exhausted finalizer deferrals `J`, the native
metadata envelope is approximately
`4320*I + D + 19*N + W + S + 8*R + 5*F + C + J` reads/day, before the existing
artifact/admission/worker/projection protocol for newly admitted units. The scan
term is zero when dark; `W` and `S` exclude loaded-owner routing and dark unowned
uploads. `19*N` includes registration, barrier/source privacy authorization and
atomic owner verification. `5*F` is the post-source privacy recheck. Each `J` also
writes the job and, when demand-admitted, its existing blind projection-counter
delta. Successful first claims add one owner write; matching owners add none.
CAS and transaction retries multiply these counts.

An exact first WAL proof against `K` native units budgets up to `22+17*K` point
reads including routing; an aliased replay is `14+K` before scheduler redrive.
Reusing a loaded owner removes the routing read: `21+17*K` and `13+K` respectively.
A pending native upload without an intake is eight reads with cold routing, seven
with a loaded owner. Add session lookup when discovery needs it. These alternative
paths replace normal processing; do not add both complete paths. Subtract
`2*sync_admissions` for removed duplicate profile reads. Existing worker/projection
and recovery redrives retain their own costs.

These are source-derived formulas, not measured daily traffic or a billing
receipt. The existing caller-attributed Firestore budget must accept actual daily
volume before any future enablement. There is no new client polling loop.

### Terminal empty recognition in the record lane

The default-off `BATCH_PIPELINE_TERMINAL_EMPTY_DISPOSITION_ENABLED` permits a
`no_recoverable_speech` result only after positive strict VAD, a GPU padded retry
that actually ran and returned empty, and an empty independent multilingual
recovery. The worker persists `worker_empty_evidence.padded_retry=empty` under
its lease only when the padded retry ran; units exceeding `min(120,
max_padded_seconds)` (>116s) skip the GPU padded retry and persist
`padded_retry=skipped_oversize` (`padded_retry_empty=False`), so CPU recovery
still attempts strict VAD / multilingual STT on them without terminal-completing
an empty multilingual response. CPU recovery pins the paid provider before
dispatch. This terminal empty span is distinct from silence,
adds no transcript words and no transcription usage, and releases successful
siblings through normal projection/client completion. The encrypted artifact
retains VAD and closed exhaustion evidence; correctness metrics count its captured
duration under `no_recoverable_speech` and shared fallback telemetry is degraded.
A completed upload may include `no_recoverable_speech_segments`; it does not claim
that these spans were silent. Uncertain/provider/validation failures remain held,
and no further paid intent is granted. The one-owner historical operator and old
GPU-canary VAD/explicit-epoch-2 route are documented in
[`batch-pipeline-serving.md`](batch-pipeline-serving.md#terminal-empty-recognition-dark).


### Dark batch-covered live preview

`BATCH_LIVE_STT_RETIRE_PERCENT` defaults to `0` and uses the exact unsalted batch
admission bucket. Before the first live socket, listen requires the prepared
conversation's durable `record_lane_owner=batch_live_v1`, matching conversation
ID, current batch/live admission, private-cloud sync, platform keys, single
channel, a declared TDT-supported language with a fully supported expected-language
profile, and pusher enabled. Resumed legacy
records never qualify just because the owner is admitted. The already loaded
conversation snapshot is reduced to scalar eligibility fields; transcripts and
photos are never retained by this gate. A client-id create that loses the
idempotent race (another writer created the id after the probe) reads the stored
row once and snapshots it, the same as a resume, rather than pinning the socket
ineligible. Fresh capture resolves the privacy level
through the existing write resolver before stamping ownership, so E2EE remains
excluded and the write performs no second privacy lookup. No new Firestore scan
is introduced.

Selected sessions prefer configured `parakeet-window`, reusing the resident
self-hosted TDT model. The internal preference is passed only by this dark gate;
existing client engine hints keep their previous semantics. Window allocation
and configured targets remain authoritative (the checked-in prod allocation is
100%). No RNNT model load or new GPU deployment is proposed.
Provider/model/language policy and the managed chain's health, account, capacity
and failover gates remain authoritative; vendor failover is still available.
Admission is pinned before a cap-paused return and stays fixed on resume.
While the retirement percentage is positive, the entire eligible population
(treatment and controls) is excluded from the separate live-demand experiment.
Controls use normal configured routing; treatment prefers TDT windows. At zero,
the previous live-demand behavior is preserved. Parity capture refreshes route
and request metadata before the first provider event, retaining cap-paused
client audio. Selection is applied once per connection. There is no silent/none-preview mode. Batch ownership and final
transcript projection are unchanged. Unknown or unsupported language profiles
retain existing live STT.
Compatible listen **and pusher** artifacts and all-live batch qualification must
precede this ramp; Cloud Run deployment alone does not install GKE artifacts.

`omi_batch_live_stt_capture_audio_seconds_total{batch_cohort}` counts decoded
capture; `omi_batch_live_stt_provider_wire_audio_seconds_total{provider,batch_cohort}`
counts successful paid PCM writes, including replay, with admission pinned before
dialing. Labels are only `admitted`, `eligible_control`, `ineligible` plus provider.
Compare Soniox wire seconds / capture seconds between arms and before/after ramp;
wire hours are `/3600`, not vendor acknowledgements or an invoice. Missing series
or zero capture exposure cannot establish savings. Paid failover remains visible
in the admitted arm. Disabling the ramp takes effect on reconnect; drain existing
WebSockets before claiming rollback complete.

`FIRST_SUMMARY_DEADLINE_PERCENT=0` admits only explicit `FIRST_SUMMARY_UID_ALLOWLIST`
owners (an omitted or empty allowlist at zero percent admits nobody). Positive
values add the same percentage bucket to the retained summary allowlist
independently of batch admission, under `FIRST_SUMMARY_DEADLINE_ENABLED`. A
positive percentage with an empty summary allowlist is cohort-only; `100` admits
all owners. Keep existing allowlists when ramping or rolling back.
Production config sets `FIRST_SUMMARY_DEADLINE_ENABLED=true` and
`FIRST_SUMMARY_DEADLINE_PERCENT=100` on backend-listen, pusher, backend,
backend-sync, backend-sync-backfill and backend-integration. This prevents
fleet traffic outside a canary from retaining the 600-second trailing settle
and 1,800-second first-summary lease/repair policy. Later revisions and
effects-only obligations retain their existing coalescing policy. The global
enabled flag is the kill switch; setting the percentage to zero restores the
explicit allowlist cohort.

The scheduled backend-sync sweep is held off in production
(`SUMMARY_DEBT_REPAIR_ENABLED=false`, deferred 2026-10-20). When enabled, it admits at most ten eligible completed server-minimum rows per two-minute
tick, rotating a page of at most 100 rows behind a global lease. It respects
manual edits, deletion, privacy, BYOK, active owners and transcript revisions;
it neither infers historical title provenance nor overrides finalization jobs.
Setting the repair flag to false stops only that scan.

A lease-admitted finalizer processes its own completed server minimum before
finishing its job, using the same summary gate and existing retry/fanout fences.
It still skips a completed row that already contains a summary or is excluded
by the repair safety policy. `finalization_status=queued` on a conversation is
an admission projection, not current job state: inspect its durable job and
actual output presence before diagnosing missing summaries.

The 120-second budget is dispatch, not model completion.
With the same master flag on, both sync and finalization enrichment adapters
use the existing `postprocess_executor`, matching ordinary conversation
finalization. Long transcription work occupies `sync_executor`; sharing that
pool made an on-time summary task wait behind STT after HTTP dispatch. No pool
size, Cloud Tasks queue capacity or instance count is increased. The master
flag off restores the prior sync-pool adapter on both routes.
`omi_accept_to_summary_seconds` can include speech arriving after acceptance.
`omi_closed_to_summary_seconds` measures from the later of acceptance and the
persisted `finished_at` (last speech), excluding that overlap. It includes the
silence-close delay; it is not a separately recorded close-transition timestamp.
Unknown acceptance or future/missing finish timestamps emit no new sample.

The dark [durable expiry custody guard](runbooks/sync-two-lane.md#durable-expiry-custody-dark)
adds `SYNC_DURABLE_REDRIVE_GUARD` and `SYNC_LEGACY_COMPLETION_CONVERGENCE`
(code/dev default false; enabled on prod `backend-sync` and `backend-sync-backfill`). Expired accepted requests move atomically into durable
`redrive_required` custody unless the content ledger already proves completion;
missing-job worker ACKs retain staged bytes. Scheduler gauges expose unresolved
custody and its nearest source/attention deadline. Legacy retries can converge
completed ledger results under their existing Redis run lock before provider work.

### Dark worker empty-ASR silence disposition

`BATCH_PIPELINE_WORKER_SILENCE_DISPOSITION_ENABLED` defaults to `false` on
worker processes (`worker.workerSilenceDispositionEnabled: false` in the
batch-pipeline-worker Helm chart). After empty ASR and its existing padded
retry, the worker reuses integration's `recovery_disposition.inspect_audio`
strict local Silero VAD and `silence_payload`. Only validated zero-speech
results persist as silence through the normal leased worker commit, with
`words_provider=strict_local_vad`, empty speech spans and zero speech seconds.
No quarantine or external provider dispatch is introduced for those results.
Speech-positive, malformed or unavailable VAD retains the existing empty-ASR
quarantine/multilingual recovery route. Positive ASR with no owned words does
not qualify. The flag does not change admission, integration recovery gates,
or paid-call authorization. Rollback sets the worker flag to `false`.
`batch_worker_silence_disposition` records persisted local silence results;
`omi_fallback_event` records `batch` → `none` / `recovered` at the same boundary.

Private user rollout membership on Cloud Run, listen and pusher uses the shared
[Firestore cohort cache](runbooks/rollout-cohorts.md). `OMI_ENV_STAGE` selects the
separate dev/prod membership maps in the customer data plane. Cloud Run manifests
omit private UID keys so live env survives a merge; Helm keeps every legacy
allowlist until a separate removal PR after live seed and verification.
OIDC sync tasks and delayed inline sync work await the first/expired cohort read
before selecting lineage routing. Dream pass and model-turn admission, pusher
shadow finalization, mobile batch renewals and
live-demand handovers also warm membership before deciding; warm checks make
zero reads. Live audio archive snapshots each phase decision for an admitted connection
to avoid packet gaps at cache expiry; cohort changes apply to that archive
connection on reconnect. Membership changes do not require a Helm env override. Seed the intended members
in their original environment before deploying the cohort-aware image and its
matching manifest/chart together. Preserve dev absent-lineage allow-all and
the live iMessage policy. Cutover order is seed, verify, switch reads, then
remove env in a separate PR.

### Deterministic batch worker validation disposition

`BATCH_PIPELINE_WORKER_VALIDATION_DISPOSITION_ENABLED` defaults to `true` and
ships on in the worker Helm chart. A Pyannote frame wholly beyond a tiny real
buffer may complete as `silence` only after independent strict local VAD
validates the real PCM, including a partial final window. Other closed worker
validation failures stop GPU retries at attempt one, retain `worker_failure`
stage/reason and enter the existing fenced recovery route. They preserve audio,
privacy generations, accepted paid intents and ordered projection fencing;
the held item does not block later results. Empty ASR on speech and positive
context words outside ownership never establish silence by themselves.
Set `worker.workerValidationDispositionEnabled=false` to restore bounded GPU
retries. Current debt counts only jobs still in `state=quarantined`; historical
quarantine timestamps and counters include subsequently recovered results.
Details: [worker runtime](../diarizer/batch_pipeline_runtime.md).

### Batch worker GPU skips (speech-only diarization, short single speaker)

Two switches decide whether the batch worker runs diarization on the whole
unit. They never change ASR input, words or speech accounting.
`BATCH_PIPELINE_DIARIZE_SPEECH_ONLY_ENABLED` (on) diarizes the union of
unpadded VAD speech and every emitted word clock, each padded by 1 s, with
nearby regions joined and 0.5 s of zeros between retained pieces; turns map
back to capture clocks. Units that would keep over 80% of their audio
(`reason="kept_over_80pct"`), empty compacted output for a unit with words, and
any compaction or mapping error (`reason="other"`) diarize the full unit and
record `omi_fallback_total{component="vad",from="batch_speech_only_diarization"}`.
`BATCH_PIPELINE_SHORT_SPEECH_SINGLE_ENABLED` is **off** by default: when on,
all speech and words forming one region of at most 2 s (pauses up to 0.5 s
inclusive) take the existing `skipped_single` `SPEAKER_00` result. Enable it
only after a paired prod-revision run qualifies it. Silence units (no VAD
speech) already skip ASR and diarization. Set
`worker.diarizeSpeechOnlyEnabled=false` to restore full-unit diarization.
Details: [worker runtime](../diarizer/batch_pipeline_runtime.md).

### Legacy sync double-empty recovery (dark)

`SYNC_EMPTY_MULTILINGUAL_RECOVERY_ENABLED` defaults to `false`. When enabled,
legacy sync keeps its bounded empty-result confirmation, then validates the
local WAV and uses the same `recovery_disposition.inspect_audio` strict local
VAD as batch recovery. Only an affirmative zero-span result is silence.
Positive spans use one independent Modulate multilingual dispatch (`attempts=2`
disables client retries), with funded sync admission rechecked before dispatch.
The owner-scoped `sync_empty_recovery` intent is keyed by the existing stable
segment identity; it has no application TTL. The sole paid call is claimed with
atomic create and its encrypted response is persisted with a version precondition
before parsing/assignment. A replay reuses the saved response; an unresolved
intent fails closed without redispatch. Positive VAD after an already-multilingual
double-empty stays retryable without a third call.
Nonempty recovered words enter the existing ordered assignment and durable
segment checkpoint path. VAD/decode errors, malformed responses, empty alternate
recognition, or empty postprocessing remain retryable failures and preserve
retry material. Existing cap-recovery intents retain their separate budget and
reconciliation path. This flag does not route jobs into batch or change intake.
Rollback: set the flag to `false`; no historical completion is rewritten.

While enabled, `sync_empty_strict_vad` logs the acoustic verdict **before** funded
admission or multilingual dispatch: `speech`, `silence`, or `inspection_error`.
It includes lane, provider, a stable HMAC segment reference, full segment duration,
strict span count and speech seconds; it never includes owner IDs or content.
Deduplicate by lane/segment reference within one release window and retain unknown
inspections separately. Speech / valid inspections measures strict-VAD-positive
double-empty segments even when recovery later fails; it is not ASR accuracy.

### Dark batch projection follow-ups

`BATCH_PIPELINE_HEAD_PRIORITY_ENABLED` (default `false`) prioritizes a ready
capture-order head within its existing scheduler class using indexed pending-head
and ready FIFO-predecessor queries (independent of backlog depth);
duration, eligible unit kind and privacy are checked before any splice, so a
head this pool cannot lease leaves FIFO intact. A 64-owner lookup budget bounds
transaction work; it does not limit positions searched within an owner. The
`head_not_found_total` and `head_budget_exhausted_total` counters plus
`predecessor_ready_age_seconds` histogram use only priority/release/namespace
labels. New ready-predecessor indexes must be READY before deploying this code.
Strict ordered merge remains authoritative; compute remains concurrent per owner.
`BATCH_PIPELINE_ASYNC_PROJECTION_ENABLED` (default `false`) restricts the batch
owner claim to ordered multipart assignment. Persisted receipts then drive
bounded tracked async bridge/playback/enrichment follow-ups, including a
post-playback redirect check for late bridge audio. Each coordinator has a
120-second deadline and at most two seconds for advisory-claim cleanup; timeout
frees the slot and retries the durable receipt on a later sweep. All siblings still need
ready receipts before atomic usage/content/client completion. Both controls
are independent of new admission; rollback reuses the same queues and receipts.
See [projection tail controls](batch-pipeline-serving.md#dark-projection-tail-controls)
for restart, privacy, ordering and p95 qualification limits. This changes no
client wire fields or live STT ownership rules.


### Paid quarantine recovery request lifetime

Each serving sweep attempts up to `BATCH_PIPELINE_RECOVERY_DISPATCH_BATCH_SIZE`
candidates across cohorts (default 16, supported range 1–64; malformed values
use 16). Held rows and dispatch errors consume the same budget. The global
query cursor and rotating allowlisted cohorts keep later work discoverable.
Set the value to 1 to restore the former all-admitted dispatch rate. This only
changes discovery throughput: `RecoveryStore.start` still fences paid intent,
and ordered merge still owns per-owner result order.

A quarantined unit with recovery state `held`, `prepared`, or `running` polls
as `processing` with `poll_after_ms: 3000`, including during admission rollback.
Terminal quarantine states such as `failed_held` still report `failed`.
Privacy-blocked polling returns HTTP 409 with `sync_batch_privacy_blocked`,
using the same response as upload.

`BATCH_PIPELINE_RECOVERY_TASKS_ENABLED` defaults to `true`. The 20-second
serving reconciliation loop prepares the existing fenced recovery intent and
queues a named HTTP task on `BATCH_PIPELINE_RECOVERY_TASKS_QUEUE` (falling back
to `SYNC_TASKS_QUEUE` when unset), using `SYNC_TASKS_INVOKER_SA`. Production
integration declares `batch-quarantine-recovery`; the existing backend deploy
action provisions it before the integration revision, with 16 concurrent
dispatches, eight dispatches/second and five attempts. This keeps accepted-work
recovery independent of legacy sync queue saturation without changing the paid
intent or provider budget. Dev retains its existing queue. To roll back queue
isolation, clear `BATCH_PIPELINE_RECOVERY_TASKS_QUEUE` on integration; keep
request-based recovery enabled. Queued wakes in either queue remain fenced.
The target is `BATCH_PIPELINE_RECOVERY_TASK_AUDIENCE` (integration's own HTTPS
origin, declared separately from the worker audience in dev and prod) plus
`/v2/batch-pipeline/recover-quarantine`; the audience must be an HTTPS origin.
The handler awaits the sole provider call and result persistence before replying,
so ordinary Cloud Run revision draining sees an active request. Background sweeps
enqueue prepared work or reconcile running artifacts. Any dispatch failure
(including missing configuration or a lost enqueue acknowledgement) falls back
to inline recovery with the same token. The task and inline path race through
the same transactional start fence, so at most one can invoke paid STT. The
shared fallback metric records this degraded inline execution. A disabled
handler host or owner cohort returns retryable HTTP 503, never a successful ack.
Each prepared intent has at most one outstanding wake task. The sweep leases a
wake on the job (`recovery_wake`, default 600 seconds,
`BATCH_PIPELINE_RECOVERY_WAKE_LEASE_SECONDS`) and names the task by intent token
and wake sequence (`-w<seq>`); while the lease is live the sweep reports
`pending` and enqueues nothing. At lease expiry the sweep probes the previous
task (`named_task_exists`, BASIC view): a task still waiting in a saturated queue
keeps its lease, and only an authoritative NotFound mints the next sequence.
Three consecutive failed probes mint one anyway, so a broken probe cannot strand
the intent. A delivery of that same wake that leaves the intent
prepared, or a failed enqueue, re-arms a 120-second wake; a stale delivery never
shortens a newer wake. `0` restores the legacy two-minute wake-name generation on
every sweep. Neither path authorizes a second provider call. Existing privacy, lease, content ownership and intent-token
fences apply to every delivery, including duplicates and admission rollback.

Integration declares a 3,600-second Cloud Run request timeout in both manifests,
above the 1,500-second task dispatch deadline and application path timeout.
Modulate has a 300-second client budget. If the application returns 504,
cancelling the ASGI await does not stop its executor thread: that thread may
still persist the result while its instance survives. A retry adopts the same
`running` token and only reconciles artifacts; it never starts a second paid
call. Ordinary drain protection ends with the request; a timeout followed by
instance death can still leave uncertain work held (including the existing
900-second abandonment fence). This is not permission to reset the token.

Kill switch: set `BATCH_PIPELINE_RECOVERY_TASKS_ENABLED=false` to restore inline
sweeping. Already enqueued tasks still use the same intent fence. No historical
`failed_held` intent is reset. An exceptional instance kill can still leave an
uncertain paid call held; this requires provider result reconciliation or an
explicit new epoch, never blind retry. Do not weaken the quarantine-to-completion
gate or exclude censored held work to hide this residual obligation.

### Sync finalize target recovery

`SYNC_FINALIZE_TARGET_RECOVERY_ENABLED` defaults on (unset/blank); explicit off or
an unrecognized token restores strict donor-refresh failures. After transcript
assignment and audio persistence, donor refresh follows only the server-authored
`sync_merged_into` returned by the rejecting transaction, with at most 16 targets.
Deleted/missing targets without a successor complete without refresh or donor
retraction. Other refresh errors defer cleanup, preserving donor tasks and the
pending revision receipt; later appends retry it. Audio copy remains strict.
The transaction still rejects writes into deleted conversations. Bounded
`sync_bridge_refresh` reasons expose degraded completion. Remaining internal
finalize errors are retryable `sync_finalize_failed`, provider/model `unknown`,
with a class/site-only `sync_finalize_exception` diagnostic. Deleted-target
acknowledgement belongs only to the legacy post-persistence callback. The shared bridge helper keeps its string-or-superseded contract;
synchronous and async batch callers retain their privacy/receipt fences. Roll back recovery with
the kill switch on every sync-intake service; classification remains internal.

Production routing-off rollout (2026-10-18): backend-listen restores static
selection with `STT_ROUTING_MODE=off`; recovery and account quarantine remain
enforced. Rollback and baseline outcome watches live in
[the routing runbook](runbooks/live-stt-routing.md#production-routing-off-rollout-2026-10-18).
Transport-open and settlement are independent: their arithmetic difference is
an accounting diagnostic, not a serving-failure abort.

### Projection discovery progress

`BATCH_PIPELINE_PROJECTION_PAGINATION_ENABLED` defaults to `true` (kill switch:
`false` restores oldest-100 discovery). Assignment and async follow-ups each
advance their own bounded 100-row keyset scan across pending, persisted and
ready results. The cursor uses `(committed_at, document ID)`, so equal timestamps
and deletion of the cursor row cannot pin a page. End-of-scan wraps to the head;
restart begins at the head without dropping any durable obligations. Follow-ups
launch the whole bounded page before fetching the next one. Discovery order is
only a hint: existing transactional predecessor, privacy, all-sibling readiness,
receipt replay and paid-intent fences remain authoritative.

The query reuses the declared prod/dev results composite index:
`projection_state ASC, committed_at ASC, __name__ ASC` in
`database/sync_pipeline_indexes.py` and the generated `firestore.indexes.json`.
No new index or production index mutation is needed.

With accepted-work serving enabled, committed pending/persisted/ready debt is
rediscovered automatically after deployment; no one-time kick is needed for the
page deadlock. `quarantine_held` obligations still require their ordinary fenced
recovery to succeed; pagination neither retries uncertain paid intents nor
resolves genuine held quarantines. Monitor falling pending age/count and durable
content/client completion on the deployed release before expanding admission.



### Static-routing first-text comparison

Ship the instrumented binary with prod listen on/100 first. After at least
60 minutes on that image, a separate stage-1 change keeps `STT_ROUTING_MODE=on`
with `STT_ROUTING_ON_PERCENT=95` (approximately 5% static managed accounts),
pinning `image_tag` to the same instrumented image.
`omi_stt_routing_cohort_window_first_text_seconds{routing_arm}` mirrors the
existing Parakeet-window first-speech-to-first-emitted-text timing once per
emitting leg. Socket construction pins the existing intent-to-treat arm through
background work and provider recovery. Labels are only `off`, `shadow`, `on`;
unmanaged sockets do not contribute. Other providers' first-text timing remains
outside this window metric. Existing histograms are unchanged. Stage gates,
sample floors, inverse percentage direction, and same-image rollback are in
[Live STT routing](runbooks/live-stt-routing.md#static-routing-cohort-ramp-stage-1).

### Default-on free-tier capability fold (2026-10-18 ruling)

`cloud_screen_vectors` is versioned in the plan catalog: ladder Free and Plus
lack it; Pro/Architect and every recognized paid legacy price retain it. Legacy
Plus is distinct from ladder Plus. The subscription response exposes the additive
`cloud_screen_vectors` boolean from the real subscription, independent of the
synthetic BYOK display plan and processing allowance. An unknown paid allowance
still waits for server admission; capability is never a funding signal.

The Gemini proxy refuses `embedContent`/`batchEmbedContents` with structured
402 `plan_gated`, `trigger=screen_context`, `upgrade_plan=operator` only when
`X-Omi-Local-Embeddings: 1` is present, the account lacks that capability, and
`CLOUD_SCREEN_VECTORS_EMBED_REFUSAL_ENABLED` is not exact `false`. Absent or
unknown headers preserve old-client behavior. Validated Gemini BYOK is exempt.
Generate/stream gates retain their existing policy.

When ladder budget admission or halt-at-cap denies processing, a valid schema-v1,
transcript-hash-bound `client_processing` summary is a display projection only.
The raw transcript and model-free title remain server-authored. The row is
`processing_deferred=true`, `deferred=false`, `status=completed`: visible but
waiting in the explicit-choice backlog, never a terminal deterministic minimum.
No provider, canonical memory/task extraction, app fanout or first-open obligation
is launched. Summary-vector indexing skips budget-waiting rows even after reset;
automatic merges cannot consume them as donors or predecessors. No summary retains the same raw deferral. Unchosen retries, refresh
and reset preserve both projection and backlog; only the existing month-scoped
choice or fitting accepted upgrade intent permits funded processing.

`CLIENT_SUMMARIES_AT_CAP_ENABLED` defaults on; exact `false` rejects new summaries
on from-segments/finalize/late-bind ingress and keeps ordinary cap deferral.
Stored projections survive rollback and processor persists omit them so a stale
worker cannot overwrite a newer ingress mutation. The old basic embed/eager gates,
local-processing flag and UID cohort are retired; their env values have no effect.
Upstream retired memory suppression, leaving no backend cohort consumer;
`FREE_TIER_EMERGENCY_STOP` and the unused backend control plane are retired too.
The existing remote `free-tier-kill-switch-v1` still covers macOS local inference
through its client adapter; it grants no cloud entitlement.

### Terminal batch assignment supersession

When sync assignment raises `SyncAssignmentSuperseded`, the batch projector
persists a ready part receipt with `disposition=superseded` and zero speech usage.
It writes no conversation content and schedules no playback or enrichment for
that part. Multipart and sibling completion still use the ordered cursor and
content ledger; successful parts retain their usage. Completed client results
include `superseded_segments` with zero failures, rather than reporting silence.
A fresh transaction rechecks privacy/deletion fences, immutable binding and
ledger ownership before settlement; a concurrently committed assignment receipt
wins. Account/protection revocation still follows the existing erasure path.


## Cap-held live audio and durable redrive

Listen owns denied PCM before provider replay. Its sink accepts at most 15 seconds
of volatile PCM, persists short encrypted fragments and composes each continuous
minute into one immutable held source. It freezes staging before composition;
expired staging and post-publication fragment cleanup resume from server sweeps.
Live immutable uploads use generation-zero puts and one transport retry, each
with a 1.5-second socket inactivity timeout and a 4-second async wait deadline
(including encoding, executor queueing and 412 verification). The upload wait
therefore returns within 8 seconds plus event-loop scheduling. A late worker
owns only upload/scratch; only the coordinator can proceed to the unchanged
Firestore transaction. A 412 is adopted only after generation, bounded size and
opaque capture metadata verification. Unknown outcomes retain their identity,
futures and any known generation; recovery never deletes an uncertain source.
The 8-second bound covers uploading one fragment, not an entire flush: summary
initialization, capacity, receipt transactions, recovery, sealing, queue draining
and lock waits retain their existing budgets.
Single-channel cap decisions can arrive out of capture order: queued provider
suppression callbacks may precede the receiver's decision for older PCM.
`OrderedCapture` assembles the already-deduplicated denied spans by integer
capture sample before encoding or allocating a source group. Only adjacent
samples with the same owner, rate, UTC month and compatible wall projection
join. The queue shares the existing aggregate 15-second PCM bound; pressure,
close and explicit barriers drain it immediately, and its original monotonic
age survives transfer into the encoder buffer. Periodic updater ticks do not
publish isolated short callback runs before the bound/age. This prevents
arrival-order clock discontinuities from minting single-part source groups.
The fragment kill switch retains the original capture writer. Direct append
paths (including multichannel) keep their transition-tail grace below.

Transition/resume force-flushes give PCM tails below 250 ms a one-second grace
from buffer opening. Compatible capture can join before encoding; channel,
conversation owner, rate, UTC year/month and the existing 100 ms continuity
check still partition buffers. Real boundary changes publish the old tail;
close and custody pressure bypass grace. The one-second updater publishes an
expired deferred tail even without more audio. Normal 15-second custody and
four-fragment/minute limits are unchanged. `omi_held_content_transition_tail_total`
has only `outcome=coalesced|sealed`: a deferred tail either receives compatible
capture or is queued without a neighbour. Short final audio remains valid.

V4 purge retains `terminal_receipts` on the existing
`users/{uid}/stt_cap_holds/{hold_id}` tombstone. Schema version 1 contains `pieces`
(an array of opaque SHA-256 piece IDs, `outcome`, source `seconds`, `attempt`,
`provider`, and `conversation_ids`) plus the union `conversation_ids`.
Outcomes are `transcribed`, `settled_empty`, `empty_plan` or `failed`; an empty
VAD plan gets one source entry with attempt 0 and provider null. Ingest proofs
are re-read before purge because they may be newer than the coordinator's row.
Only allowlisted fields survive: no audio paths, text, provider results, plans
or encrypted metadata. The same tombstone retains publication fragment IDs and
its existing lifetime (no TTL; owner account erasure removes it). A sibling
collection would split that barrier/retention authority without adding value.
Legacy non-v4 erasure is unchanged. Failed reconciliation still retains audio.
A final summary checkpoint writes `seconds=0.0` whenever count becomes zero,
in the same fenced transaction; positive-count summaries keep fractional seconds.

Pusher receives admitted audio/transcripts and has no duplicate cap-held PCM sink.
Both lifespans await the lightweight sink registry before background drain/HTTP
teardown; socket close flushes tails. GKE grace is 120 seconds with a 15-second
preStop and a 60-second uvicorn session-drain bound, leaving 45 seconds for the
30-second custody flush plus existing 10-second drain. The flush coordinator
returns after its wait bound without gathering cancellation-resistant closes;
storage-executor publication leaves retain scratch/PCM until actual IO finishes,
even if event-loop shutdown cancels the async publication task. Process hard
termination can still lose the at-most-15-second undurable tail.

`POST /v2/cap-hold-recovery/sweep` uses sync Tasks OIDC and awaits bounded work.
Production Scheduler calls it every two minutes; independent persisted discovery
cursors visit processing choices, abandoned staging and cleanup. Five owners run
concurrently on existing executors, each considering up to 100 items over bounded
ten-row pages with a monotonic 900-second next-item/next-piece budget. Durable
intent remains processing after a budget stop; a pre-cutoff unsealed source keeps
that choice open until sealing preserves its original creation/listing times. Only existing
same-month user/upgrade consent may process audio. Existing UID/hold/text leases,
cutoffs, reserved estimates, abuse and allowance checks still own admission.
`STT_CAP_RECOVERY_COHORT`, when present, additionally intersects effective dispatch
membership for consent/upgrade, API enqueue, redrive owner visits, worker entry,
manifest recovery and every provider attempt. It shares dispatch's `uid:`, `pct:N`
and `all` grammar; unset leaves existing scope unchanged and malformed (including
empty) denies recovery. It never changes listen-side hold admission or staging
seal/cleanup. The private probe identity is supplied through the private-values
path, never committed; see [recovery-only probe narrowing](stt-cap-dispatch.md#recovery-only-probe-narrowing).
Process death cannot erase the choice or require a client wakeup. The fixed
60-second memory wakeup and processing self-respawn are retired.

Held STT uses one provider POST per persisted dispatch attempt and encrypts
nonempty provider results before ingestion; restart reuses those results and the
original provider/model. The whole proposed document cache budget is 768 KiB;
a usable oversized or aggregate-budget-miss response proceeds directly to ordinary
ingestion without caching, preserving earlier caches. Its crash uncertainty lasts
through atomic ingest proof. No size-only failure forces another vendor call. A kill
between vendor acceptance and the result checkpoint can still repeat a paid call.
The [durability runbook](runbooks/cap-hold-durability.md) owns the two-attempt bound,
retained uncertainty, kill switches, three new group indexes, deployment order,
15-second SIGKILL residual window and measured cost increase. Enforcement flags
remain off; this source change does not activate the cap cohort.

## Operator minimal sync redrive

Operator redrive payloads may carry `redrive_mode: 'minimal'` to re-transcribe preserved legacy sync audio at minimum cost: Parakeet-only pre-recorded STT with no Modulate recovery, attach-first assignment to a compatible row within 10 minutes through the normal lifecycle owner, and one gpt-5-nano title per newly created row instead of conversation processing. Details and the kill switch live in `backend/utils/sync/ARCHITECTURE.md`.

Runtime STT-cap and ladder execution scope shares the 60-second, environment-fenced
Firestore token cache; session admission does not wait for it. Existing live sessions
retain admitted state until reconnect. See [runtime ramp and rollback](stt-cap-dispatch.md#runtime-cohort-no-restart).

### Silent operator held-unit redrive

The paid epoch-2 operator can freeze an owner/time/ID-scoped held-only cohort and
use `--silent`. The intent and assignment receipt retain that choice; assignment
stamps `sync_silent_redrive_revision` transactionally with the transcript.
Enrichment for that exact revision regenerates notes while deferring derived
reminders/notifications and creation webhooks. Silent projection retains donor
cleanup debt rather than cancelling client reminders. Later ordinary revisions
resume ordinary processing. Serving and enrichment code must be deployed before
paid silent apply; CLI-only rollout cannot guarantee silence. See
[the scoped redrive runbook](runbooks/scoped-batch-redrive.md) for digest approval,
canary/remainder prefix semantics and stop conditions.

### Stale reconnect recovery

Reconnect recovery claims at most ten `in_progress` rows older than one hour,
newest eligible first. A per-user `conversation_recovery_state/stale_in_progress`
CAS cursor advances before attempts, including refusals/errors; value cursors
survive row deletion. Subsequent reconnects page toward older rows, wrap after
the tail, and wait at least one minute between page claims. Each call reads at
most two ten-row pages plus cursor/deletion-marker state. Concurrent reconnects
cannot claim the same cursor generation. The claim transaction checks the durable
account-deletion marker before writing, so an erasure cannot be followed by
recreated cursor data. Query, cursor and marker share the customer data plane.
A process crash can postpone its claimed page until the next pass, but cannot pin
later pages. Recovery remains reconnect-driven.

Discarded, deleted, locked and deferred rows remain protected. The outcome log
has only the conversation ID and a bounded disposition; it has no UID/content.
`omi_stale_recovery_outcome_total{outcome}` distinguishes accepted handoff/empty
deletion, not-admitted, error, oversized-close attempt, cancellation and current-session skip.
Unexpected metric inputs fold to the shared reason enum’s `unknown` bucket.
Acceptance is not worker completion. `STALE_IN_PROGRESS_RECOVERY_ENABLED=false`
is the default-on feature's kill switch; it disables this reconnect sweep only.
The new descending status/activity composite must be READY before shipment.

### Approved historical stuck-row backfill

Historical recovery uses the immutable `silent_backfill_v1` job policy and the
`SILENT_BACKFILL` processing trigger. It shares normal recovery admission and
lease fences but persists summary/title only, with no derived-work or delivery
queues. Retries and completed replays remain silent. Noise closes are fenced,
metadata-only, retain transcript/audio and have a commit-revision-bound restore.
Operator plan, kill switch, owner/global pacing, receipts and approval sequence:
[stuck backfill runbook](runbooks/stuck-backfill.md). Shipping these seams starts
no scan or redrive; applying any private plan requires separate authorization.

## Durable mono pendant/phone quiet checks

`LISTEN_QUIET_FINALIZATION_ENABLED=true` is the default. Mono `omi` and `phone`
recordings arm one opaque `quiet-*` check in `conversation_finalization_jobs`
on session setup and every socket exit (including drain/network failure).
Arming on setup covers process death before teardown, including rows that gain
content later. Checks leave the conversation `in_progress`; they do not bind
`finalization_job_id`, increment the processing revision, or emit side effects.
The first due time is the persisted last-content time plus the configured quiet
timeout, including the stored calendar continuation window. The ordinary
120-second gap is the fallback for legacy rows lacking the timeout stamp.

At each wake, the check transaction re-reads the conversation and its recording
session lease. A continuing owner or later content reserves a new wake at the
later of the quiet deadline and lease expiry. An expired owner admits content
through the normal transactional `SERVER_RECOVERY` lifecycle path with
`require_cloud_tasks=True`. That admission rechecks status, content, protected
output, locks, discard/deletion, quiet time and the live lease. A reconnect
between evaluation and admission therefore wins; a reconnect with a new
conversation identity does not suppress the old row's obligation. Desktop
clean-close and multichannel processing keep their existing paths. Their
existing job bindings also fence any quiet check of the same row.

Only opaque job ID and dispatch generation enter Cloud Tasks. Session setup
stamps only a boolean BYOK requirement; credential-free recovery creates a
blocked BYOK job, never substitutes platform credentials. Each check
reserves its next generation before processing admission, so worker death,
duplicate delivery, task exhaustion and enqueue failure retain indexed replay
debt. Normal processing jobs retain their existing revision/lease/fanout fences.
The periodic outbox reconciler uses a 60-second default while this switch is
on (300 seconds when off). It owns due wakes for GKE listen, which keeps its
existing inline pusher route and needs no finalization queue binding to persist
checks; queue-capable producers can additionally schedule opaque Cloud Tasks. The independent fast in-progress sweep is eligible after five minutes
while this switch is enabled, using a due-content collection-group index (status, has_content, finished_at)
instead of rotating through historical empty stubs, with its bounded 2,000-row scan and
ten-admission tick cap; historical backlog drain time remains bounded by those
caps, not guaranteed for the whole fleet in a single tick. The original two-hour full scan remains always on, with its own cursor and
five-minute scan cadence, for markerless content and unknown legacy leases.
New recordings have
a per-conversation due item and do not depend on that legacy scan or a reconnect.

`omi_conversation_orphaned_unfinalized_total` is an unlabeled counter of observed
quiet, content-bearing rows without a processing job and without a live lease
(including legacy-sweep admissions; the conservative two-hour fallback also
counts rows whose old recording lease is unknown). Alert on a positive increase over a short
window such as ten minutes; retries can observe the same row more than once.
This measures missing finalization handoff, not missing capture or STT ingress.

Deploy the queue-capable Cloud Run API/reconciler/worker consumer code before
the GKE listen producer for minute-scale recovery and metric acceptance. A
producer-only rollout retains durable indexed custody, but recovery wakes and
the orphan metric wait for the consumer revision to serve; treat that interval
as a partial rollout. Keep the existing Cloud Run durable-finalization
queue/handler bindings; GKE listen retains inline finalization. Verify check creation, deferral for
same-conversation continuation, a single processing revision after lease expiry,
and the alert signal before declaring runtime acceptance. The declared due-content index must be READY before rollout; the existing
Firestore readiness release gate enforces that prerequisite. Historical redrive
is not part of this change. Kill switch:
`LISTEN_QUIET_FINALIZATION_ENABLED=false` on **all** those hosts; this suppresses
arming, retires queued checks without processing, and restores the prior
2-hour fallback threshold. Already-admitted processing jobs finish under their
normal fences. Re-enable with `true`; resumed sockets and the legacy sweep pick
up remaining debt. This PR does not deploy or change any production row.

### Held-audio staging transport custody

A live held fragment keeps the same immutable GCS identity across at most two
upload attempts. Each socket timeout is 1.5 seconds, each async wait is at most
4 seconds, and the whole upload window is 8 seconds including exponential
backoff with scaled uniform jitter: `min(0.2 * 2**(n-1), 1.0) * U(0.5, 1.5)`
seconds. With two attempts the one backoff is 100–300 ms. Retryable errors
are transport timeouts/resets, HTTP 408/429 and 5xx. Other HTTP 4xx are permanent
and do not retry. Encoder workers finish before upload workers start; late
upload workers retain encoded bytes only and cannot commit a held receipt.
Both encoder passes retain their original 20-second subprocess timeout; the
8-second budget applies to upload waiting, not encoding. The outer shutdown
delivery deadline still bounds the close waiter.

After an exhausted or permanent upload, the receiver keeps its PCM until an
owner-fenced, encrypted emergency journal is created in the existing
`stt_cap_holds` staging collection. The journal contains at most 128 KiB of
encoded Opus plus encrypted metadata, has no TTL, and expires its writer lease
immediately. The existing staging sweep restores the same immutable fragment,
then appends and seals through the fragment-claim ledger. That claim prevents
an ambiguous original staging commit and journal recovery from counting the
same audio twice. With fragmentation disabled, the journal uses the original
receipt ID and a separate recovery fragment ID. A permanent GCS error leaves
the journal for later sweeps after the underlying permission/request fault is
fixed; it cannot trigger a tight retry loop on the live socket.

Pending PCM is limited to 15 seconds per session (60 with fragmentation off)
and 600 seconds across a pod. Reaching either bound forces a flush/handoff
before accepting more capture; no chunk is evicted. If both storage paths fail,
ordinary flushes retain their PCM for the next update. At a full bound or final
close, an unconfirmed handoff fails closed: 1011 is reserved for inability to
continue custody. All updater exceptions are contained and logged; the updater
continues until the session stop event. Sealing/refund failures after custody
transfer do not revoke the durable record or close the live socket.

Client close and lifespan shutdown flush pending publication through the same
journal path. The delivery budget is shared with listen's
`SHUTDOWN_DELIVERY_SECONDS` (28 seconds); the lifespan caps its wait at that
budget. Already deferred fragments need no GCS availability on close: the
journal survives the socket/pod and the scheduler owns recovery. Unconfirmed
bytes remain process-owned until publication completes; a simultaneous failure
of both durable stores cannot be converted into a success acknowledgement.

Ramp signals: `omi_held_content_staging_retry_total{outcome}` has the closed
outcomes `retried_ok`, `deferred`, `exhausted`, `permanent`, `retained`.
`retained` increments on each non-final flush that cannot reach either durable
store and keeps PCM volatile; it is distinct from a confirmed `deferred` journal.
Treat any new `retained` increment as custody at risk even below the memory
limit. Its WARNING is emitted at most once per hold per 60 seconds; updater
and publication-failure warnings use the same per-hold interval independently.
`omi_held_content_deferred_seconds_total` counts seconds handed to journals;
`omi_held_content_pcm_custody_seconds` measures current process-local held PCM.
It refreshes on capture/retirement and each one-second updater tick. It is an
occupancy signal, not a heartbeat or durable backlog: idle held PCM does not
expire, so an unchanged value is valid. The cap guard should use `retained`
counter increases for new volatile-custody failures, rather than infer health
from whether this gauge changes.
The existing publication counters and fleet staging/redrive snapshot remain
available. No user/session/object identities are metric labels.

## Capture/speaker/notes refactor integration

The integrated composition and rollback contract is documented in
[capture/speaker/notes refactor](capture_speaker_notes_refactor.md). The app-client
surface now includes authenticated coverage queries and boundary-evidence intake;
wire generation owns OpenAPI, Dart, Swift and TypeScript outputs. Live speaker
suggestions include explicit `is_user`, and committed capture events preserve
channel, epoch and recording-session identity. These additive fields are ignored
by clients until their opt-in paths serve.

Live durability → coverage ledger → committed event is the custody ordering.
Continuation uploads stage encrypted chunks by deterministic session and index,
retain incomplete/expired assemblies for client retry, and only return a completed
receipt after durable projection. Tombstone-first deletion purges staging even
without a conversation parent; seven-day expiry is checked at assembly, with
physical Firestore TTL activation required before serving.

Merge jobs share the finalization outbox. Admission is atomic across every source;
epoch renewal fences stale workers. Correction transfer atomically seals the
irreversible boundary, so recovery either rolls back preparation or replays only
source cleanup. Empty quiet checks retain their durable successor until the
lifecycle owner's content/lease-fenced delete succeeds. Notes/episode prompts use
L2 roster priors and L3 rendering/language authorities; notes cache identities
include effective packet selection and version. Cloud Run metric export includes
the refactor families while retaining the prerecorded-language gate allowlist.

### Wave 2 evidence and consumers

Speaker refinement uses the same evidence record: count hints cannot merge
confident distinct identities, and desktop-channel/near-field anchors remain
explicit candidates. Live text-only introductions cannot create or link people
in the speaker serve cohort. Unknown acoustic evidence preserves the existing
identity/status; count consolidation does not require an identity claim.

`MEDIA_NOISE_MODE` classifies before paid notes, using current speaker evidence
and bounded foreground screen observations. Its serving minima persist
`content_kind=media|noise` and the existing terminal-no-derived-effects marker.
Memory/task entrypoints and daily/simple/comprehensive recaps use the same
eligibility helper. Restoring a recording protects it, reclassification clears
its exclusion, and successful reprocessing clears the terminal marker. Shadow
preserves transcripts, labels and derived output; it never writes media edges.

Cross-device reconciliation runs at completed-conversation finalization. Wall
time proposes candidates; ordered shared speech must confirm every group pair.
Grouping preserves original transcripts and audio, and a secondary with any
unique speech remains visible. Exact secondary capture keys acquire only
already-committed frame aliases to the elected primary. Coverage queries
transactionally revalidate fingerprints, privacy state, membership and primary;
edits, separation or primary replacement invalidate old aliases. No wall-time
position is converted into a source-frame receipt.

`coverage.committed_events` can construct confirmed alias receipts, but live
secondary-socket dispatch and streaming partial-window suppression remain
unwired. Closed clients query coverage. Server SNR/confidence is optional and
not consistently persisted; absent measurements retain source/duration ranking.
This branch does not repair historical derived effects or release audio based
on inferred alignment.

Database-writing shadow paths use `refactor_modes.shadow_sampled`: capture
coverage, speaker evidence and boundary evidence each declare
`<FLAG>_SHADOW_PERCENT`, default 5. Serve cohorts always write; zero disables
shadow writes. No serve percentage or digest cohort is configured in source.


Wave 2 call projection preserves optional `meeting_link`,
`call_controls_detected` and `call_platform` through the boundary payload.
Phone transports, matching calendar meeting links and explicit joined controls
can establish call metadata; media playback and an open chat window cannot.
Conversation locations remain owner locations, with remote participants explicit.

V4's boundary publication is serve-only under `CALLS_RECAP_MODE`; shadow
projects phone/calendar evidence locally and never adds it to the wave-1
boundary authority for non-cohort users. Soniox initial, reconnect and managed
legs pass the authenticated request uid explicitly to vocabulary admission;
no ambient BYOK identity is consulted. Social tasks with a named counterpart
and a specific place/time survive the owner-alias guard.

Scheduled recaps retain the completed-day grace period. An explicit on-demand
request bypasses that grace to summarize the partial day, while keeping locks,
compute admission, content guards and durable state handling.

V4's completed-local-day recap producer shares `status` and `generated_at` with
M's read adapter. Pending, failed, empty and not-generated rows keep a null
`generated_at`; only a legacy successful row without that field uses creation
time as a compatibility fallback. Stored media/noise-only days are not-generated,
not empty capture days. Rollback resumes unfinished stubs under the same ID.
Both one-shot and agentic notes mounts receive cohort-gated owner-involved task
rules, authenticated identity and matching cache identity. Exact-reference stale
capture policy still lacks its producer binding/CAS lifecycle adapter; no fuzzy
or historical task cancellation is performed.
